## Opportunities by region

🔵 **Placement rule applied throughout:** region comes from a **stated institution**, never from a
personal name or a language hint (`p545`). Rows that cannot be placed that way are left
**UNPLACED** rather than guessed.

### North America

🔴 **A measured absence, and it is the headline for this region.** Across all **140** rows of the
`moodle mcp in:name` field, **zero** name a US or Canadian institution — while APAC names seven
and EMEA ten. 🔵 **The reading this base can defend** is not that North American universities
lack agentic LMS access, but that **they are not building it in public under this name**, which
is consistent with the region's demand-side picture already on this base (district AI policy
mandates, procurement of commercial tutors such as the `$10M` Khanmigo earmark). 🟢 **Opportunity:
the build-vs-buy gap is widest here** — the region procures finished tutors while other regions
publish integration code, so a studio engagement in North America starts with **no local open
precedent to reuse** and should price integration work accordingly rather than assuming a
community artefact exists. 🟡 Measured on name/description text only → `564-C`.

### EMEA

🟢 **Ten institutions named**, the densest *regulatory* and now second-densest *supply* region:
Univ. of Bath, ASE Bucharest, ONPU Odesa, MIREA, FH JOANNEUM, ETH Zürich, NAK, Zewail City
(Egypt), a Swiss target, and one multi-target covering UCL/KCL/LSE/Cambridge/Warwick.
🔴 **But the EMEA grant state is the worst of any region**: its one graded head,
`AmritSanjeev2007/UoB-moodle-mcp`, is **GPL-3.0** (`35 149 B`, `3972dc9744f6`), and its one
vendor entrant, **Enovation**, is an **empty name reservation**.
🟢 **Opportunity:** the **partner layer is staking Moodle MCP plugin names before shipping**, so
an EMEA engagement should expect the client's existing Moodle Partner to be the incumbent on the
integration surface — and should propose the `Teci-AI` fork-vs-call shape (GPL plugin + separately
licensed server) that keeps Globant's deliverable clear of the GPL.
🟡 Zewail City (Egypt) sits at `0★`, so it **confirms rather than refutes** `p563`'s finding that
there is no African-origin open-source education agent above trivial stars.

### APAC

🟢 **Seven institutions named — the densest supply region on this base**: Waseda, Nagoya Inst. of
Technology, Tokyo Univ. of Technology, NCCU (Taiwan), UNNC Ningbo ×2, IIIT Hyderabad, Monash.
🟢 **And it holds the only permissive grant found this pass** —
`12dora/waseda-moodle-mcp`, **MIT**, `1 063 B`/`d3dc53f9558a`.
🔴 **Three of four APAC addresses carry NO grant at all** (IIIT Hyderabad, Monash, Nagoya), with
code present and no licence file — unusable as-is however good the code.
🟢 **Opportunity:** APAC is where a studio can actually *reuse* rather than rebuild — one MIT
reference implementation plus six sibling institutions with the same problem. 🔵 The concrete
move is to consolidate them, and the **unlicensed three are a relicensing conversation, not a
dead end**: single-author university repos whose authors are identifiable and reachable.

### LATAM

🟢 **Three institutions named — UTN (Argentina), Unisinos (Brazil), CETEC** — all already held by
this base, and 🔴 **nothing new arrived this pass**. 🟢 The standing position is unchanged from
`558-H`'s correction: the *"no LATAM-origin supply"* claim remains **refuted** (12 addresses,
Brazil 8, Argentina 4), but **no new LATAM-origin permissive education repository surfaced**.
🔴 **This is the third consecutive pass in which LATAM supply did not grow.**
🔵 **A correction to this base's own standing directive.** `p561`–`p563` concluded that *"the
marginal leg is worth more in LATAM and more in supply than in demand anywhere."* 🟢 The
**supply** half held and paid this pass — but it paid in **APAC and EMEA**, not LATAM. 🔴 So the
LATAM half of that directive has now failed to pay on three consecutive attempts, and the honest
restatement is: **supply is the right axis; LATAM is not where supply is currently being
created.** 🟢 **Opportunity, unchanged and still real:** `i-educar` and the Brazilian/Argentine
cluster remain the region's reusable base, and LATAM's instrument supply is still being created

## 🆕 `p563` — `2026-10-31` — **🟢 EMEA'S AFRICAN HALF IS NO LONGER UNPROBED: the per-country leg `p562` named as the right instrument was run, and it paid — named deployments, one peer-reviewed, all commercial.** 🔴 **And the code leg NILLED: there is no African-origin open-source education agent above trivial stars.**

🟢 **`p562` wrote that EMEA *"is not an African blank plus a European shelf — it is a European
shelf with an unprobed African half, and the right instrument is a per-country leg."* That leg
ran this pass.** It returned six facts, 🟢 **none of which this base held** (`M-Shule`,
`AltSchool Africa`, the `8.1 %` adoption figure, the six-country UNESCO pilot, Nigeria's NDEPS
and the `250 000` learner figure all returned **zero** prior hits across all eight files).

### 🔵 The architectural reason the open-source shelf does not reach this market

Africa's shipped AI-in-education runs on **SMS and WhatsApp**, not on web applications — a
channel chosen for 2G reach, feature phones and intermittent connectivity. 🔵 **That is a
delivery-channel constraint, and it is why the agent shelf on this base — which assumes a
browser, a modern runtime and often a GPU — does not intersect this market at all.**
🟢 **An agent that speaks SMS/WhatsApp against a low-bandwidth budget is an unserved shape, and
it is the one build this region's evidence actually calls for.**

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🔴 **Nil new supply this pass — fifth consecutive measured blank, recorded rather than left
silent.** The leg returned Gallup's 60 % teacher-use figure, the 134 bills across 31 states, the
Maryland/Idaho/Oklahoma/Virginia mandatory-district-policy quartet, `NY A 9190`, the US DoE
supplemental priority of `2026-04-13` and the K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act — 🔴 **all
already shelved.** 🟡 The standing opportunity is unchanged: governance is the stated barrier,
not capability, so **the sellable artefact is the policy-and-evaluation layer**, not another
tutor.

### EMEA

🟢 **The pass's new supply, all of it. European half: no new instruments — the regulatory picture
is stable for a sixth consecutive leg** (`Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`, Annex III education
→ `2027-12-02`, Annex I → `2028-08-02`, Article 50 transparency live since `2026-08-02`).
🟢 **African half, probed first-hand per country:**

| Player | Country | What it is | Grant |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M-Shule** | 🟢 Kenya (Nairobi), operating since **2017** | SMS + WhatsApp + Telegram + web learning platform. AI drafts quizzes, recaps and five-minute *"Refresh"* sessions. Delivered to refugees in **Kakuma** and **Dadaab**, farmers in Tanzania, and families across **30+ Kenyan counties** | 🔴 **commercial — no open-source licence found** |
| **AltSchool Africa** | 🟢 Nigeria | Commercial digital learning platform; **20 000** students enrolled from Nigeria, Kenya, Ghana, Rwanda and the UK as of Sep 2023. CEO **Adewale Yusuf**. **"AI for 10M Africans"** initiative; AI-literacy network reported at **50+ pilot centres** across Ghana, Nigeria, Uganda and Kenya serving **250 000+ learners** | 🔴 **commercial** |

🟢 **M-Shule carries the strongest evidence base of any artefact on this leg, in either
half:** a peer-reviewed study in the ***British Journal of Educational Technology*** (2025)
found that literacy material delivered through its SMS platform **improved learning outcomes**.
🔵 **That is a randomised-evidence claim for an AI-assisted delivery channel, and this base holds
no European or North American equivalent** — the rigour is in Africa, and the shelf had none
of it.

🟡 **Founder attribution is unresolved and is recorded as such:** Tracxn gives *Julie Otieno,
2016*; an October-2026 article gives *Claire Mongeau*. Two sources, two names, not reconciled —
carried as conflicting, not adopted.

**Policy, per country:**
- 🟢 **Kenya** — National AI Strategy **2025–2030** names education and skills development, proposing teacher training and AI content in schools. 🔴 **Generative-AI adoption reported at `8.1 %`**, behind South Africa. 🔵 **The binding constraint is named as implementation, not policy**: a rural teacher without reliable connectivity, devices or training cannot act on a strategy document.
- 🟡 **Nigeria** — the **National Digital Economy Policy and Strategy (2020–2030)** references AI, but education use is assessed as **aspirational rather than systemic**.
- 🟡 **South Africa** — draft national AI policy; its **education pillar covers STEAM curricula and community AI centres**. (This base already carries the `2026-04-10` publication / `2026-04-27` reported abrogation pair, still unconfirmed on two readings.)
- 🟢 **UNESCO** is piloting **AI-readiness assessments across six Southern African countries** to guide national AI-in-education policy. 🆕 New to this base.

🔴 **And the code leg nilled, explicitly.** A GitHub API search for African-origin education
agents (`Kenya`, `Nigeria`, `Africa`, `Swahili`, `curriculum` across name, description and
readme) returned **35 results of which not one was an education repository** — the ranking
filled with crypto and MCP repositories matching unrelated tokens. 🔴 **There is no
African-origin open-source education agent above trivial stars.** 🟢 **So EMEA's African half is
now *characterized* rather than unprobed:** real deployments, commercial, channel-first, one
peer-reviewed, and **open-source-absent**. That is a finding, not a gap.

### APAC

🔴 **Nil new supply — every instrument returned was held.** South Korea's AI Basic Act
(`2026-01-22`), **Vietnam's Law on AI in force `2026-03-01` with an 18-month transition for
education**, China's registration and content-labelling regime with penalties to ¥50 M, India's
AI-content labelling (`2026-02-20`), Japan's voluntary posture, Singapore's AI Verify, and the
Ipsos Education Monitor 2026 ban-support movements (Japan 21→29 %, Australia 47→52 %, Korea
27→31 %, Indonesia 30→23 %) — 🔴 **all already shelved.** 🟢 APAC remains the region with the
most *code* supply on this base (`Echo-Loop`, `Wisepen`, `AI-Marker-Suite`, `kaogong-shuati`),
and all four were held too.

### LATAM

🔴 **Nil new supply — a measured blank, stated rather than inferred.** The leg returned UNESCO
IESALC's 87 % / 74 % / 26 % / 9 % figures, the LAC Observatory (launched `2026-04-14`), the
Universidad de los Andes count of **716 regulatory instruments across 23 countries**, PISA 2025's
nine-in-ten chatbot use across 13 countries, and Brazil's 53 %-of-administrators / 22 %-with-
guidelines split — 🔴 **all already shelved.** 🟡 The standing LATAM reading is unchanged and
still the sharpest opportunity statement on this base: **adoption is high, governance is
absent** (26 % with a strategy, 9 % evaluating), so the sellable artefact is **evaluation and
governance**, and the region's shipped open-source strength remains **school management**
(`portabilis/i-educar`), not agents.

### 🟢 `562-E` — the only thing the demand side paid, and it is a partition not a discovery

🟢 **This base carries the US Department of Education's final supplemental priority on advancing AI
in education as *published* `2026-04-13`.** 🟢 This pass's leg returns the same instrument as
**effective `13 May 2026`**. 🔵 **The two do not conflict — they are different events** — and the
distinction is the operative one for a proposal, because a priority governs discretionary grant
competitions **from its effective date**, not its publication date.

🔴 **Source class named, per `557-D`: this is a search-result summary, not the Federal Register.**
🟢 So it is recorded as a **candidate partition with its citation attached**, not adopted — the same
discipline `p560` used on Article 50, which paid off, and `557-D` used on the bill counts, which
also paid off. 🔵 Closure needs one primary read of the Federal Register notice, which this host can
reach.

### 🟢 `559-D` CLOSED — the scope words, and what they explain

🟢 **Registered as:** *"record each firm's scope word in the ledger beside its figure, so the spread
can be decomposed instead of merely declared."* 🟢 **Done, in the [Global](#global) child, for ten
firms.** The headline result:

| | `558-F` / `p559` | 🆕 `p560` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| firms | 8 | 🟢 **10** |
| 2026 base spread | $3.7 B → $11.4 B = **3.08×** | 🔴 **$1.94 B → $11.4 B = 5.9×** |
| terminal spread | $12.3 B → $136.8 B = 11.1× | unchanged |
| CAGR spread | 12.55 % → 40.9 % = 3.3× | 12.55 % → 41.5 % = **3.31×** |
| 🔵 **status of the spread** | *declared* — "the firms count different things" | 🟢 **decomposed — the spread is ordered by scope breadth** |

🟢 **Two new rows, and both are informative rather than merely additional.**

- 🔴 **MarketReportsWorld ~`$1.94 B` (2026)** — a **new low outlier**, roughly half Persistence's `$3.7 B`, and its scope is the narrowest found: **intelligent tutoring systems as one application category**. 🟢 It is the bottom of the range *because* it is the narrowest cut, which is the decomposition working.
- **Market.us `$8.9 B` → `$73.3 B` (2033)**, scope **solutions + services** — lands in the upper band, with the upper band's scope word.

🟢 **And one publisher supplies the mechanism in its own words.** Precedence Research's notes say
its figure *"depends on market boundaries and how embedded AI revenue is treated"*. 🔵 `559-D`
corrected `558-F`'s belief that the firms shared scope words; this is a publisher **agreeing in
print** that it does not share them.

🔴 **One firm also disagrees with itself**, which is `560-G`'s mechanism on the money axis: Research
and Markets / Business Research Co.'s `$10.6 B → $42.48 B (2030)` carries a CAGR of **41.5 %** in
one place and **40.9 %** in another. 🟢 Recorded as a range, not reconciled.

🟢 **Operative consequence, and it is stronger than `558-F`'s:** the prohibition on a single total
is now **absolute and justified rather than precautionary**. A midpoint across incompatible
boundaries is not a noisy estimate of a real quantity — **there is no single quantity**. 🟢 Quote
**segment rank order** (K-12 first at ~34 %) and name the firm, base year and scope word, or quote
nothing.

can manufacture false saturation.**

### 🔴 `559-D` — why the market total disagrees, and why that makes the rule stronger

🔴 `558-F` found a **3.1×** spread on the 2026 base and called it *"same quarter, same scope
words"*. 🟢 **The scope words were not the same.** This pass's leg returned the mechanism from the
sources: *the gap comes from differing definitions, since some estimates count only dedicated AI
education software and others include tutoring, services, and embedded AI.*

🔵 **This is a better reason for the same rule, and it kills the obvious workaround.** If the
spread were measurement noise, an average or a midpoint would be defensible. 🔴 Because it is
**definitional**, the midpoint of *"software only"* and *"software + services + embedded"* measures
**nothing that exists**. 🟢 `558-F`'s prohibition therefore holds absolutely: **no single total in
any deck, summary or pattern** — segment mix and rank order only.

🟢 **`Gap 455-F`'s mechanism, third occurrence.** A quantity with a wide cross-source spread is
retired rather than averaged. 🔵 It applied to *"134 bills / 31 states"* (2.6×), to the market
total (3.1×), and this pass declined to record a US state count at all when the same leg returned
*"~34 states plus DC and Puerto Rico"* and *"more than 28 states plus DC"* **in the same session**.

ordered below it but their shares are not consistent enough across firms to publish.

### 🔴 The channel asymmetry, re-characterised — and seventeen passes described it wrongly

🔴 `p455` recorded `www.iesalc.unesco.org` as refused at **CONNECT** (`connect_rejected`) by the
network policy, and seventeen passes since have recorded demand nils as **saturation**.
🔴 **Measured directly this pass: the host does not resolve at all** —
`getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND www.iesalc.unesco.org`. That is **DNS**, not a connect refusal: the host is
outside this environment's allowed domains.

🟢 **And in the same session, WebSearch returned that study's findings with quoted figures.**
🔵 **So the demand leg is `search`-reachable and `fetch`-unreachable**, and the two are different
transports with different reach. 🔴 **Seventeen "saturations" were measured on the transport that
cannot reach the host, in a session where the transport that can was available.** 🟢 That is why
LATAM paid this pass after seventeen blanks — not because the channel moved, but because the leg
was run on the channel that answers. Registered as `557-F`. 🔵 It is the same error class as
`Gap 554-A`, where `p554` tested `curl` alone and generalised to "the platform channel is gone".

🟡 **What this does not license.** A search summary is still a **relay**: it carries quotes, not
bytes, and this base cannot verify a quoted figure against a primary it cannot fetch. 🟢 The
mitigation stands — publish the **instrument number** (`Gazette Notice 3880 of 2026`,
`Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`, `K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026`, `PL 2338/2023`) so a
reader with primary access can check what this base could not.

### 🔴 The LATAM supply blank is real, and here is the sweep that proves it

🔵 `p553` through `p555` recorded LATAM as a supply blank. 🔴 A blank that is never probed in
the region's own languages is indistinguishable from an instrument that cannot see it. 🟢 **So
this pass probed it**: `educación OR educativo OR educação OR docentes tutor IA
in:name,description,readme stars:>20`, sorted by stars, MCP transport.

🔴 **73 results, and not one is a LATAM education artefact.** The top fifteen are a hiring list
(53 008★), a Linux software list, a security PoC collector, a vision-transformer paper list,
three copies of an anti-propaganda corpus, awesome-lists, an n8n workflow repository in Spanish
(`DragonJAR/n8n-workflows-esp`, 527★ — 🔵 LATAM-origin and Spanish-language, but automation,
not education) and a Brazilian C/C++ study-material collection (`cppbrasil/material-de-aprendizado`,
441★ — 🔵 LATAM-origin and educational *content*, but not an AI artefact and not a platform).
🔵 **Reading `readme` as well as name and description is what produces the noise**: the words
appear incidentally in hundreds of unrelated repositories.

🟢 **Verdict: the blank is a property of the population, not of the instrument.** The one
LATAM-origin education artefact this base holds remains
[`GuilhermeScarpini/agente-bncc-professores`](https://github.com/GuilhermeScarpini/agente-bncc-professores)
(MIT, Brazil, BNCC/MEC-grounded, 🔴 **0★**), shelved at an earlier pass. 🔵 **LATAM's position
in this market is as a demand centre with world-leading adoption rates and essentially no
published open-source supply** — which is precisely the gap a studio with LATAM delivery
capacity is positioned to fill, and it is stated here as an opportunity rather than as an absence.

🔵 `p554` received two APAC curriculum leads from a single low-reliability aggregator with no

#### 🆕 `p549` — regulation thickened, supply did not move for a tenth pass

🔴 **No new NA supply artefact — tenth consecutive pass.** The leg

🟢 **What is new and citable is that the regime has moved from guidance to *mandate* in a
countable set of states**, which changes what a district engagement must ship:

- 🟢 **Four states now require both state-level guidance *and* district policy adoption** —
  **Maryland**, **Idaho**, **Oklahoma**, **Virginia**. Maryland's package also calls for
  district **AI coordinators**, i.e. a named human owner.
- 🟢 **New York `A 9190`** limits classroom AI use to **grade 9 and above**, with exceptions
  for diagnostics and special education. 🔵 **This is an age-gate in statute**, and it is the
  first NA instrument on this base that a product must enforce in code rather than in policy.
- 🟡 **`K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026`** advanced out of House committee —
  🔴 **not law**, recorded as pending.
- 🔵 **NYC** issued preliminary guidance (**March 2026**) on a traffic-light framework;
  **Berkeley Law** barred AI from exams and credited coursework from **summer 2026**.
- 🔵 The student-drafted **`STUDENTS FIRST Act of 2026`** (all 50 states, via AASA) proposes
  AI-literacy instruction, no AI on graded assessments, and a **right to appeal an
  AI-detection finding**. 🔴 A proposal, not law — but the appeal right is the clause a
  vendor would have to build, so it is worth tracking.

🟢 **The opportunity, stated as work:** NA's binding constraint is now **enforceable policy
in the product** — per-grade gating, a named-owner audit trail, and an appealable
AI-detection decision. 🔵 The agent shelf has exactly one artefact shaped like that
([`anthropics/k12-teacher-skills`](https://github.com/anthropics/k12-teacher-skills), **605★**
re-measured this pass, because it ships **eval rubrics** rather than prompts), and the
platform layer that would carry the audit trail is **not** NA-origin. 🔴 **That asymmetry is
the NA gap:** the regime is the most prescriptive of the four regions and the open-source
supply answering it is the thinnest.

guidance and district policy — is already held on this base. 🔵 Seventeenth pass in which the

🟢 **Supply side, new and measured:** the two strongest **MIT**-licensed LMS-class platforms on
these shelves — [`learningequality/kolibri`](https://github.com/learningequality/kolibri)
(**1 162★**) and [`ls1intum/Artemis`](https://github.com/ls1intum/Artemis) (**823★**) — plus
[`openedx/XBlock`](https://github.com/openedx/XBlock) (**471★**, Apache-2.0).

🟢 **The opportunity is procurement-shaped, not engineering-shaped.** US districts and
universities are the market where a licence review is a **gate**, and this pass measured that
three shelf platforms return `NOASSERTION` on the field every scanner reads — `OpenOLAT`
(Apache-2.0 verbatim), `thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem` (Apache-2.0 verbatim) and `pupilfirst`
(MIT verbatim). 🟢 **Deliverable:** a one-page licence attestation per platform, generated by
[`../ingest/operative-body.py`](../ingest/operative-body.py), that proves the operative body is
present when GitHub will not say so. 🔵 That is a billable artefact in a market with an
**April 2026 ADA accessibility deadline** already forcing platform review.

🔴 **And one recommendation to re-check before any NA engagement:**
[`instructure/canvas-lms`](https://github.com/instructure/canvas-lms) (6 892★, AGPL-3.0) has a
public mirror **not pushed since `2026-04-30`** — six months. 🟡 Not abandonment; Instructure
develops internally. But AGPL-3.0 plus a six-month mirror gap is a combination a client must be
told about before it is designed around.


#### 🆕 `p556` — the permissive SIS exists, it is MIT, and it is 410 stars

🔴 **Demand leg saturated for a tenth consecutive pass.** `AI education North America 2026
adoption regulation players` again returned the **`134 bills / 31 states`** figure as its top
organic result — 🔴 **retired at `p455`, refused again here**, for the sixth time. 🔵 The other
recurring numbers (60 % or 61 % of US teachers using AI tools, attributed to Gallup; 55 % of
high-school principals not restricting access) arrive only through aggregators and are **not
adopted**.

🟢 **Two regulatory facts worth carrying, both structural rather than numeric:**
- **Federal:** the **`K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026`** advanced out of the House
  Education and Workforce Committee. 🔵 An AI-*literacy* mandate creates curriculum and
  teacher-training demand, which is a content-and-enablement engagement, not a platform one.
- **State:** four states — **Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma and Virginia** — are reported to require
  **both** state-level guidance **and** mandatory district-level AI policy adoption. 🟢 That
  pairing is the billable shape: a district policy obligation without a policy instrument is a
  procurement event in every district in those four states.

🟢 **The supply finding, and it inverts this region's usual shortlist.**
[`academico-sis/academico`](https://github.com/academico-sis/academico) (**410★**, 151 forks,
🟢 **MIT**, pushed `2026-10-28`, Laravel + Filament) is **the only permissive student
information system on this base** — and `openSIS-Classic` (347★, 288 forks), the row a US K-12
shortlist actually reaches for, carries 🔴 **no licence file at all**: `spdx_id` `NONE`,
default copyright, on a repository whose own description says *"commercial grade, secure,
scalable"*. 🔴 **It must come off any shortlist**, and `academico` is what replaces it.

🔵 **And the region's two largest LMS platforms are both `AGPL-3.0`** — `openedx` (8 213★) and
`canvas-lms` (6 892★), the latter 🔴 **183 days without a push** on its open mirror while the
vendor ships SaaS. 🟢 **North America's permissive play is therefore the SIS and the
assessment layer, not the LMS**: `academico` (MIT), `jupyter/nbgrader` (BSD-3-Clause, 1 370★)
and `ucbds-infra/otter-grader` (BSD-3-Clause, 161★, UC Berkeley) are all `BUILD-ON`, and all
three sit in exactly the Jupyter/Canvas/Gradescope estate a North American university already runs.


🆕 **`p557` (`2026-10-30`).** 🟢 **Instrument and adoption figures, relayed:** the **US Department
of Education published its final supplemental priority on advancing AI in education on
`2026-04-13`**; **30 states** had issued AI-in-education guidance as of January 2026; Gallup puts
**60 % of US teachers** using AI tools, and a College Board figure has **55 % of principals** not
blocking generative AI on the school network. 🔴 **The `134 bills / 31 states` figure surfaced
again and is again NOT adopted** — `Gap 455-F` retired it as one tracker among four disagreeing by
2.6×.
🟢 **Opportunity, and it moved against us this year:** **Instructure shipped an agent product in
March 2026**. A Canvas-replacement pitch now argues against a *shipped* product rather than a
roadmap, which is a materially harder sale. 🟢 **The counter-position is the permissive base:**
[`sakaiproject/sakai`](https://github.com/sakaiproject/sakai) is **`ECL-2.0`** (**11 120 B `raw`**, re-read `p562`; 🔵 the `11 119` this line carried is `raw − 1 newline`, the convention `560-A` retired, and read this
pass) — the Educational Community License is what HE consortium procurement already recognises, and
`BUILD-ON` means the deliverable can be the client's property. 🔵 Usage is ahead of governance here,
so the sellable first phase is **policy-plus-pilot**, not platform replacement.

### EMEA

🆕 **`p562` — `2026-10-31`: 🔴 ZERO NEW INSTRUMENTS — a fifth consecutive measured blank, and a 23rd pass without EMEA supply.** The leg returned **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** (OJ `24 Jul 2026`, applied `27 Jul 2026`, Annex III education → `2 Dec 2027`), **Article 50** transparency live since `2 Aug 2026`, Dubai **KHDA**, the **OECD *Digital Education Outlook 2026***, the EC Digital Education Action Plan and the UK's light-touch posture — 🔴 **every one already shelved**. 🟢 **The regulatory picture is stable, which is itself the finding for a proposal:** `p560`'s partition — *the conformity programme is deferred to 2027, the disclosure duty is already in force* — survived a fifth independent leg unchanged, so it is safe to put in writing. 🔴 **Africa returned nothing at all, and that is recorded explicitly rather than left silent:** no African national AI-in-education policy, adoption figure or vendor reached this leg. 🔵 EMEA is **not** an African blank plus a European shelf — it is a European shelf with an **unprobed African half**, and the right instrument is a per-country leg, which `Gap 445-F` already proved pays where region-level legs nil.

🆕 **`p561` — `2026-10-31`: 🔴 ZERO NEW INSTRUMENTS — a 22nd pass without EMEA supply and now a demand leg that returned only held facts.** The leg returned **seven** instruments, all already shelved: **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** deferring Annex III education to **2 December 2027** and Annex I to **2 August 2028**; 🟢 **Article 50 transparency NOT deferred and in force since 2 August 2026** (`p560`'s EMEA find, re-confirmed by an independent relay this pass); Dubai **KHDA** guidance to private schools; **China and the UAE** as the two states running compulsory national AI curricula since the 2025-26 school year; Kenya's **National AI Strategy 2025-2030**; South Africa's draft national AI policy **published 10 April 2026 and abrogated 27 April 2026**; and the African Union's **2024 continental AI strategy**.
🔵 **Africa remains the region's thin half, and it is thin in a specific way:** the leg returns *strategies* and *directives* (Kenya's strategy, Nigeria's NERDC robotics-and-AI curriculum directive, the AU framework) and 🔴 **no deployment, no procurement and no supply**. 🟢 Both relays name the same cause — a shortage of qualified computing teachers in Nigeria and Kenya — so the constraint is **delivery capacity, not policy**. 🔵 That is a Globant-shaped gap and it is written down as one rather than left as an absence.
🟢 **The region's one new fact this pass is a supply fact, and it comes from `p561`'s instrument.** [`openedx/openedx-platform`](https://github.com/openedx/openedx-platform) — the reference MOOC base for EMEA public-sector and university deployments — ships an AGPL-3.0 with **+615 `ws` bytes of operative text that narrows §13**: a public web service speaking to Open edX over RPC or other inter-service communication is declared **not** part of the `Corresponding Source` (`561-B`). 🟢 **For an EMEA engagement that is the difference between a service tier that must be published and one that need not be**, and it is invisible to a byte-level scanner — which, under the AI Act's conformity documentation, is exactly the artefact a client will have.

🆕 **`p560` — `2026-10-30`: 🟢 THE FIRST EMEA INSTRUMENT IN TWENTY-ONE PASSES, AND IT WAS HIDING
INSIDE A DATE THIS SHELF ALREADY CARRIED.** 🔴 This base has recorded `Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`
and the `2 December 2027` Annex III deferral since `p556`. 🟢 **What it had not separated is that
the deferral does not cover Article 50: transparency obligations were NOT deferred and have applied
since `2 August 2026`.** 🔵 So the widely-reported headline *"education delayed to 2027"* is true of
the conformity programme and **false of the disclosure duty**:

| obligation | date | status today |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 🔴 **Article 50 transparency** | **2 Aug 2026** | 🔴 **IN FORCE NOW** |
| Annex III high-risk, education included | **2 Dec 2027** | 🟢 deferred from 2 Aug 2026 |
| Annex I — AI embedded in regulated products | 2 Aug 2028 | 🟢 deferred |
| Ban on emotion recognition in education | Feb 2025 | 🔴 in force since |

🟢 **Commercially this is the difference between a 2027 programme and a live obligation.** A tutor,
chatbot or content generator running in an EU school **today** owes Article 50 disclosure **today**;
grading, admissions and proctoring owe Annex III conformity by `2 Dec 2027`. 🔵 Schools remain
**deployers** under Article 29 even when the tool is a vendor's, which is why the artefact an EMEA
engagement is asked for first is a documented licence-and-provenance gate (`P560-A`), not a model.
🟢 Instrument detail re-confirmed this pass: OJ publication **24 July 2026**, in force **27 July
2026**. Enforcement splits between the **European AI Office** (DG CONNECT, general-purpose model
providers) and national authorities for everything else; funding runs through Erasmus+, Horizon
Europe and ESF+.
🔴 **No new EMEA *supply*.** The region returned regulation only; no EMEA-origin repository entered
the shelf this pass. 🔵 An informed gap, stated so the blank does not read as coverage.

🆕 **`p559` — `2026-10-30`: 🔴 NO NEW EMEA INSTRUMENT. TWENTIETH CONSECUTIVE PASS, AND THIS IS AN
INFORMED GAP, NOT SILENCE.** 🟢 The leg ran and returned substance — it was simply **all already
carried here**: `Regulation (EU) 2026/1744` (the Digital Omnibus) moving stand-alone high-risk
education obligations from `2 Aug 2026` to 🟢 **`2 Dec 2027`**; the **Article 4 AI-literacy** duty
already binding; schools and universities as **deployers**; the four high-risk education
functions (admissions/access, evaluating learning outcomes, assessing appropriate level of
education, **proctoring**); and the Commission's May 2026 ethics-guidance update. 🔵 Every one of
those probed as present on this shelf — `Digital Omnibus` 20 files, `2026/1744` 22, `December 2027`
9, `Article 4` 10, `proctoring` 115.
🔴 **What the leg did not return, in twenty passes: a single EMEA *supply* address.** 🔵 The EMEA
engagement shape on this shelf is therefore still *regulatory readiness on platforms built
elsewhere* — `ILIAS` (DE/CH, `GPL-3.0`, default branch `release_11`) and `OpenOLAT` (CH,
🟢 `Apache-2.0`, grant read first-hand this pass) are the two EMEA-origin platforms carried, and
both predate the AI wave entirely.

🆕 **`p558` — `2026-10-30`: no new EMEA instrument. 🔴 Nineteenth consecutive pass, and the leg
again returned predominantly US material.** Everything EMEA-shaped it did return — the UAE's
nationwide K-12 AI curriculum mandate, Dubai **KHDA**'s private-school guidance, the EU
**omnibus** commencement and the high-risk date dispute (`August 2026` vs **`2 December 2027`**
for stand-alone Annex III and `2 August 2028` for embedded systems) — 🔴 **is already on this
page.** 🔵 The primary host for the last of these, `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu`, is
🔴 **`getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`** from this session while WebSearch serves its substance — see
`558-G`, which is why that date dispute remains labelled secondary.

🟢 **One genuinely new EMEA observation, and it is on the *supply* leg:**
[`nzengi/dubbai`](https://github.com/nzengi/dubbai) — a Turkish-language AI tutor built to the
**MEB** (Türkiye's Ministry of National Education) curriculum. 🔴 **0★, no grant field, created
`2026-10-28`** → not promoted and not recommended. 🔵 Recorded because nineteen passes of EMEA
nulls have been about the demand leg; this is an EMEA **supply** address aligned to a national
curriculum, which is the artefact the demand leg keeps implying should exist.

🟢 **The only region that produced claims this base did not already hold.** Both are
regulatory, both are 🔴 **secondary-sourced**, and both are labelled as such because the
primary hosts are unreachable from this session (`Gap 548-D`).

🆕 **United Kingdom — Ofqual, *Principles of AI use in marking*, January 2026.** The
qualifications regulator's working paper concludes that AI is **not ready to be the marker**
in high-stakes assessment. Reported positions: using AI as *the sole or primary marker*
does not comply with Ofqual's regulations; current evidence does not support a general case
for AI in marking, with performance varying significantly by context; the paper is read
against the UK's five AI principles — **safety, transparency, fairness, accountability and
contestability** — and Ofqual's evidence to Parliament stresses that **marks must be
contestable**. 🟢 Chief regulator **Sir Ian Bauckham** is reported as writing that AI is
promising for **quality assurance and marker training** but *"nowhere near ready to take
over high stakes marking."* 🔴 `ofqual.blog.gov.uk` fails **DNS resolution** from this
session, so none of the above is a verbatim primary read.

🟢 **The engagement this licenses is unusually well-specified by the regulator itself.** The
compliant product is **not** a marking agent. It is everything Ofqual names as promising:
marker **training** simulators, QA **sampling** that flags divergent marking for human
review, and a **contestability trail** — a record of why a mark was queried that a student
or centre can actually challenge. 🔵 An EMEA assessment client who has read this paper will
reject a marking agent on sight and buy the QA layer.

🟡 **France — a lead, recorded as a lead and not as a finding.** A French *académie*
published, in **June 2026**, an *"assistant IA souverain pour les personnels de l'éducation
nationale"* — a sovereign AI assistant for national-education staff. 🔴 The host
(`ent2d.ac-bordeaux.fr`) fails **DNS resolution** here, so the tool, its operator, its
models and whether it is open source are all **unread**. 🔵 It is written down because it is
the first signal on this base of a *national education system's own staff-facing sovereign
assistant*, which is a different thing from the sovereignty-of-infrastructure trend this
base already tracks. **Next pass: resolve it or drop it.**

🔵 Held and unchanged: **Bridge AI**, the **Advanced Digital Skills** grants (€25.4m), the
**EU AI Act** Article 50 calendar, and the European Commission's updated ethical guidelines
on AI in education.

🔴 **Demand side: no new fact.** Regulation **(EU) 2026/1744** moving stand-alone Annex III
high-risk obligations to **2 December 2027** (with embedded AI to **2 August 2028**), Article 50
transparency duties from **2 August 2026**, and the Council of Europe's planned *Legal
Instrument on AI in Education* are all already held. 🔵 One unadopted lead: a Spanish-language
outlet claims the Commission approved binding AI-in-education rules on **15 April 2026**,
effective **1 January 2027**. 🔴 **Uncorroborated by every other source in the same return and
NOT adopted** — it would contradict the `2026/1744` deferral this base holds from stronger
sources.

🟢 **Supply side, and it is the sharpest regional finding this pass.**
[`OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT`](https://github.com/OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT) (**449★**) is this page's
recommended EMEA base precisely because it is **Apache-2.0** — permissive enough to carry
proprietary extensions into a public-sector deliverable. 🟢 **Proven this pass: its payload
carries the verbatim Apache-2.0 operative body.** 🔴 **And GitHub's licence field for it is
`NOASSERTION`, for two blank lines and a truncated appendix.**

🔴 **Why that bites hardest in EMEA.** The AI Act's high-risk regime requires **technical
documentation and provenance** for a deployed education system; an EU public-sector procurement
runs an SBOM and a licence scan; both read the field, and the field says nothing. 🟢 **The
engagement:** Apache-2.0 attestation plus AI Act Annex IV documentation for an `OpenOLAT`
deployment, with the licence defect fixed **upstream** — a two-line whitespace patch to
`OpenOLAT/LICENSE` would make the field resolve and is the cheapest contribution on this shelf.

🟡 **One EMEA platform to price differently:** [`sakaiproject/sakai`](https://github.com/sakaiproject/sakai)
(1 240★) is **`ECL-2.0`**, confirmed first-hand this pass against sources that disagreed. ECL-2.0
is Apache-derived but its **patent grant is scoped to the contributor's own institution** —
materially narrower than Apache-2.0 for a consultancy shipping to a third party.


#### 🆕 `p556` — the high-risk date finally has an instrument number, and the region's best content hook is a standard

🟢 **`Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`, the "Digital Omnibus on AI".** For the first time on this
page the education high-risk delay is attached to a named instrument: reported published in the
Official Journal on **`2026-07-24`**, in force **`2026-07-27`**, moving stand-alone **Annex III**
obligations — the eight areas including education — to **`2027-12-02`**, and **Annex I**
product-embedded systems to **`2028-08-02`**. 🔴 **Sourcing is still law firms and
consultancies only**; no EUR-Lex or OJ page was reachable this pass, so it is carried as
*reported*. 🟢 But a regulation number is a thing a client's counsel can look up, which the
previous five passes' bare dates were not.

🔵 **What is high-risk in education, per those sources:** evaluating learning outcomes,
screening applicants, placing learners in programmes, monitoring students during exams, and
affecting access to qualifications. 🟢 **The delay is to timing, not substance** — and
prohibited practices, AI-literacy duties and GPAI obligations did **not** move, so the
literacy obligation is live now while the high-risk conformity work has a two-year runway.
🔵 **That asymmetry is the EMEA engagement shape**: AI-literacy enablement is immediate
revenue, high-risk conformity is a 2027 programme to be scoped in 2026.

🟢 **Supply: two EMEA-origin permissive rows, and one is a dead hackathon.**
[`beltromatti/get-it`](https://github.com/beltromatti/get-it) (**1 141★**, 138 forks,
🟢 **`Apache-2.0`**, built at **GDG AI Hack Milan 2026**) is a Feynman-technique study tool with
a knowledge graph and 3D visualisation — 🔴 **123 days without a push**, so it is a pattern to
copy rather than a dependency to take. [`artcc/freelingo`](https://github.com/artcc/freelingo)
(216★, Spain, self-hosted language learning on **Ollama** with CEFR plans and voice) is
🔴 `AGPL-3.0` → 🟡 `SELF-HOST-ONLY`. 🟢 **`oaknational/oak-open-curriculum-ecosystem` (MIT,
England) remains the region's — and this base's — only curriculum-placed permissive graph.**

🟢 **The region's cheapest integration surface is a standard, not a platform.**
[`h5p/h5p-php-library`](https://github.com/h5p/h5p-php-library) is embedded in Moodle, Canvas,
Drupal and Open edX across the European public estate; an AI generator that **emits H5P** reaches
all of them without touching a line of their code — which also keeps the generator outside the
copyleft perimeter of every LMS it lands in. 🔵 For a sovereignty-sensitive EMEA client the
pairing with `freelingo`'s Ollama-local design is the obvious one: local models, standard
content format, no data leaving the estate.

🔵 **Named EMEA actors this pass, beyond the EU:** the **UAE** (nationwide K-12 AI curriculum
mandate, reported from a May 2025 Cabinet decision), **Dubai's KHDA** (responsible-AI guidance
issued to private schools), and the **UK** (principle-based, through existing regulators,
consultation ongoing — 🟡 blog-sourced). 🔴 **An informed gap: no national ministry instrument
was found this pass for France, Germany, Spain, Saudi Arabia or the Nordics**, and the search
that should have produced them returned US material instead.


🆕 **`p557` (`2026-10-30`).** 🟢 **The date split is the finding, and it changes what to sell
first.** Education's **high-risk** obligations under the EU AI Act sit at **`2027-12-02`** for
stand-alone Annex III systems (`Regulation (EU) 2026/1744`, carried from `p556`) — 🟢 **but
Article 50 transparency duties still begin `2026-08-02` and were NOT delayed.** 🔵 So the
near-term EMEA deliverable is **disclosure and transparency**, which is due now, rather than
high-risk conformity, which is fourteen months out. A proposal that leads with conformity is
selling the client's 2027 problem.
🟡 **Africa, relayed and unconfirmed:** South Africa's draft national AI policy lists education as
a priority sector — **Gazette Notice 3880 of 2026, `2026-04-10`**, reported **abrogated
`2026-04-27`**. 🔴 Two readings disagree on current status; the instrument number is published so a
reader with primary access can settle what this base cannot.
🟢 **Supply:** [`OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT`](https://github.com/OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT) is **`Apache-2.0`**
(10 982 B — 🟢 reproducing this shelf's own prior byte count exactly), deployed across Swiss, German
and Austrian institutions: the permissive EMEA counterpart to Sakai.
🔴 **Informed gap, eighteenth consecutive pass:** no national ministry instrument found for France,
Germany, Spain, Saudi Arabia or the Nordics; the EMEA query again returned predominantly US
material.

### APAC

🆕 **`p562` — `2026-10-31`: 🔴 ZERO NEW INSTRUMENTS.** The leg returned Korea's **AI Basic Act** (`22 Jan 2026`, high-impact includes education), **Vietnam**'s AI law (`1 Mar 2026`) with its **18-month education transition**, India's AI-labelling IT Rules (`20 Feb 2026`), Japan's voluntary model, Australia's proposed high-risk guardrails, the **Ipsos 2026 Education Monitor**, and UNESCO's note that China and Singapore hold established AI-in-education policies — 🔴 **all held**. 🔵 **Vietnam's exact education grace date is still refused** under `557-D`: this leg returned *"an 18-month transition"* without a date, which is consistent with the shelf's `18-month` limb and does **not** resolve the three conflicting values (`1 Mar 2027 + 6 months`, `up to 18 months`, `Sep 2027`). 🟢 Recorded as a fourth confirmation of the limb and **not** as a closure, because a transition *length* and a transition *date* are different facts and the sources only ever give one of them.

🆕 **`p561` — `2026-10-31`: 🔴 ZERO NEW INSTRUMENTS.** The APAC leg returned **eight**, all held: South Korea's **AI Basic Act in force 22 January 2026**, the region's first comprehensive AI statute; **Vietnam's Law on Artificial Intelligence from 1 March 2026**, with education among the sectors carrying a longer transition (🔴 the exact grace period is still a `557-D` refusal — three incompatible values); India's amended IT rules requiring **labels and provenance markers on AI-generated content from 20 February 2026**; India's **Class 3 AI curriculum from AY 2026-27**; China's **April 2026 AI-literacy plan**; Australia's reliance on existing law plus voluntary guidance, Japan's voluntary guidelines and Singapore's **AI Verify**; UNESCO's observation that China, Japan, the Republic of Korea and Singapore are already integrating AI curricula; and the **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026** split on banning AI in schools (support rising in Japan and Australia, lower in Indonesia).
🔵 **South Korea stays the region's most useful negative case**, and it is held: the mandatory AI-textbook programme ran ahead of readiness, adoption sat **below 30 %** by March, and the National Assembly **stripped the textbooks of official status**. 🟢 A proposal into APAC can price readiness assessment on that precedent rather than on an abstraction.
🟢 **The supply side moved here and it moved through grading, not discovery.** [`bigdata-ustc/EduNLP`](https://github.com/bigdata-ustc/EduNLP) — the region's educational-NLP base — was re-read first-hand: **11 346 raw** `8aaf2e630b1c`, 🔴 **12 bytes SHORTER** than canonical Apache-2.0, and 🟢 **substantively identical**, the whole residual being one filled appendix line (`Copyright [2020] [bigdata-ustc]`, `561-G`). 🔵 An APAC engagement inheriting a client's byte-diff gate would see a *negative* delta on a verbatim licence; 🟢 the normalised pin resolves it in one comparison.

🆕 **`p560` — `2026-10-30`: 🟢 APAC SUPPLIED THIS PASS'S BEST NEW ADDRESS AND ITS CLEAREST BLOCK —
the region is now the shelf's most productive supply source two passes running.**
🟢 **New, MIT, grant read first-hand:** [`BenyRonald77/uajy-academic-rag-chatbot`](https://github.com/BenyRonald77/uajy-academic-rag-chatbot)
— `main`/`LICENSE`, **1 068 B** `dc10763308da`, HEAD `26b04dbb168b`, **452★ / 2 forks** — a
Streamlit + FAISS + Gemini RAG chatbot over **Universitas Atma Jaya Yogyakarta's** academic
handbook. 🔵 It is supply *and* demand evidence in one address: a real Indonesian university
deployment of exactly the shape `P560-B` describes, which is worth more to a proposal than another
framework.
🔴 **And the region supplied the pass's clearest licence trap:** [`DavidDingXu/hello-llm`](https://github.com/DavidDingXu/hello-llm)
— 20 illustrated lessons, browser training, no GPU — ships **PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0**

deliverable. 🔵 Same shape as `cn-math-taxonomy` (`CC-BY-NC-4.0`) — **this region's best education
assets are disproportionately non-commercial**, and an APAC engagement should assume a licence read
is required on every candidate rather than exceptional.
🟢 **Regulation, two dated statutes, both carried with their limits:** South Korea's **AI Framework
Act** in force **22 Jan 2026** with its enforcement decree the same day — generative-AI **labelling**

Law on AI**, passed **10 Dec 2025**, in force **1 Mar 2026**, the first comprehensive standalone AI
regime in Southeast Asia, **extraterritorial**, with implementing **Decree 142/2026** of **30 Apr
2026**.
🔴 **Vietnam's education grace period is NOT recorded, per `557-D`** — three sources gave three
answers in one session: *1 Mar 2027 + 6 months* for systems operating before 15 Aug 2026; *up to
18 months* for legacy high-risk health/education/finance; *September 2027*. 🔵 An informed gap.
🔴 **Attitudes are moving against the policy direction.** Ipsos Education Monitor 2026: support for
**banning** AI in schools rose in Japan **21 % → 29 %**, Australia **47 % → 52 %**, South Korea
**27 % → 31 %**; Indonesia fell. 🔵 A regional rollout that assumes permission tracks capability is
under-pricing change management.

🆕 **`p559` — `2026-10-30`: 🔴 NO NEW APAC INSTRUMENT. SATURATED — and the probe is published so
the null is checkable.** 🟢 The leg returned the Ipsos attitudinal series (support for **banning AI

Korea's `AI Basic Act`, Japan's non-penal `AI Promotion Act`, China's service-by-service regime

(4 files) but **not that commencement date** — so the *instrument* is known and the *date* is new.
🔵 Recorded at that precision deliberately: it is a labelling duty that reaches any tutoring or
content-generation deliverable shipped into India, and a date is what a delivery plan needs.
🔴 UNESCO's regional read is unchanged — China, Japan, Korea and Singapore are building AI
curricula while others are still meeting basic education needs; 🔴 **no APAC-origin open-source
education supply address has ever appeared on this shelf.**

#### 🆕 `p555` — Indonesia's AI mandate is an ELECTIVE bolted on by regulation, not a curriculum

🟡 **`Gap 554-G` closed here.** Indonesia's school-level AI content does **not** arrive through
Merdeka Belajar. As reported by this pass's search channel — 🔴 **attributed, because
`bgtksumsel.kemendikdasmen.go.id` and `antaranews.com` are both `000` through this session's
proxy** — the instrument is **`Permendikdasmen No. 13/2025`**, which inserts a single article
between Arts. 32–33 creating **Coding and Artificial Intelligence as an ELECTIVE**
(*mata pelajaran pilihan*), offerable from **grade 5 SD / 7 SMP / 10 SMA** from the **2025/2026**
year, gradual and conditional on school readiness — and schools may remain on **Kurikulum 2013
or Merdeka** either way.

🟢 **Why the elective-not-mandate distinction is the commercially important half.** A mandate
creates a procurement; an **elective** creates a **readiness problem**, because every school
decides separately and most lack a teacher who can teach it. 🔵 Reported alongside: a planned
**Coding-AI training programme for one million teachers** (older plan, progress unconfirmed) and
**ImajiNation 2026**, a Kemendikdasmen/Assemblr EDU/Samsung visual-coding competition targeting
**11 000+ participants from 2 000 schools across 38 provinces**. 🔴 None of these is
first-hand-attested; all are search-channel returns.

🟢 **The opportunity that follows from the shape:** not an LMS, but **teacher-enablement at
provincial scale, offline-capable**. [`learningequality/kolibri`](https://github.com/learningequality/kolibri)
(**1 162★, MIT**, pushed `2026-10-30`) is the only platform on these shelves built offline-first
*and* permissively licensed — the right base for 38 provinces of uneven connectivity, and MIT
means an Indonesian ministry deployment can be extended without source obligations.

🔴 **Philippines: the lead was redundant.** MATATAG is **not** the Philippine AI instrument;
`DepEd Order No. 003, s. 2026` and `CHED Memorandum Order No. 21, s. 2026` are, and this base has
held both since `Gap 488-Q`. 🔵 Also held: Project **AGAP.AI** and the **₱100M** AI-centre
allocation; newly reported this pass but **not adopted** (host `000`): **RM No. 250, s. 2026**
rolling out *AI Ready ASEAN Philippines* to up to **1.5M** learners, teachers and parents in
2026, and **MIT assisting the DepEd curriculum review free of charge**.

🟡 **Korea / Vietnam, held, no change:** Korea's **AI Basic Act** in force January 2026 with
**MSIT** treating 2026 as a guidance-first grace year; Vietnam's decision listing **education**
among six high-risk sectors, automated assessment named.


#### 🆕 `p556` — two dated national mandates, one of them from a primary government channel

🟢 **China, and this is the best-sourced regulatory fact of the whole pass.** An **AI-literacy
action plan published April 2026** on `english.www.gov.cn` — a primary government channel, not
an aggregator — spanning *all levels of schooling and lifelong learning*: AI education
integrated into local curricula nationwide, and universities required to make AI a **basic
public course** with subject-specific textbooks. 🔵 Reported alongside it, at lower source
quality: primary pupils barred from independent generative-AI use, and teachers prohibited from
substituting AI for core instruction. 🟢 **A textbook-and-course mandate at national scale is a
content-production engagement measured in thousands of hours**, and it is the largest single
piece of named demand on this page.

🟢 **India: AI and computational thinking compulsory from Class 3 in academic year 2026-27**,
with a **CBSE expert committee chaired by Karthik Raman (IIT Madras)** designing the curriculum,
framed to **NEP 2020** and **NCF-SE 2023** under an *"AI for Public Good"* emphasis — ethics,
social responsibility and critical thinking ahead of technical skills. 🟡 Multiply reported in
trade press; the final CBSE notification was not reachable. 🔵 Backed, per the same sources, by
the **₹10 372 crore India AI Mission**.

🟡 **The rest of the region, with its instruments:** **South Korea's AI Basic Act in force
`2026-01-22`** (decrees pending, 2026 run as guidance not penalty) — and separately, Korea's
mandatory **AI textbook** plan **stalled**, with adoption under 30 % and the National Assembly
removing the textbooks' official status, which is the one *negative* adoption datum on this
whole page and worth more than three positive ones. **Vietnam's decision of `2026-07-02`**
names high-risk AI across six sectors **including education**, automated assessment among them,
with extra obligations for systems operating before `2026-08-15`. **Australia's TEQSA** requires
every higher-education provider to file an institutional generative-AI risk action plan.
🔵 **China is prescriptive, Korea is retrenching, Vietnam is sector-listing, and Japan,
Singapore and Australia are light-touch** — so a single APAC AI-education offer cannot exist;
the region needs per-jurisdiction policy nodes, which is `../compose/patterns.md`'s standing
APAC pattern.

🟢 **APAC is the supply leg again, for a fourth consecutive pass, and it supplies the top of the
shelf.** [`HKUDS/DeepTutor`](https://github.com/HKUDS/DeepTutor) (**51 608★**, University of
Hong Kong, 🟢 `Apache-2.0`, **v1.7.0 `2026.10.28`**) and
[`THU-MAIC/OpenMAIC`](https://github.com/THU-MAIC/OpenMAIC) (**41 931★**, Tsinghua, 🟢 `MIT`) are
the two largest education agents in existence and **both are `BUILD-ON`**. 🔴 But the region also
supplies the shelf's most valuable *blocked* artefact:
[`xiaoyang111222/cn-math-taxonomy`](https://github.com/xiaoyang111222/cn-math-taxonomy)
(**5 480★** in twelve days, 1 388 forks) — 1 644 knowledge points, 2 403 prerequisite edges,
6 723 items, aligned to the Chinese national curriculum — on a grant the platform cannot
classify. 🔵 **The Chinese-language tutoring cohort is also the most commercially specific one
found anywhere**: [`KeWang0622/kaogong-skill`](https://github.com/KeWang0622/kaogong-skill)
(212★, 🟢 MIT) targets the civil-service exam, and
[`3yangyang9/DeepTutor-AILearning`](https://github.com/3yangyang9/DeepTutor-AILearning) (169★,
🟢 `Apache-2.0`) is a **multi-tenant platform for offline training institutions** with
tenant→campus→teacher→student hierarchy and per-tenant model and billing policy — 🟢 the only
row on this base built for the *operator* of a training business rather than for a learner.


🆕 **`p557` (`2026-10-30`).** 🟢 **The first attitudinal series on this base, and it runs against
adoption.** **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026**, support for *banning* AI in schools: **Australia
52 %** (from 47), **Japan 29 %** (from 21 — the largest move in the series), **South Korea 31 %**
(from 27), **Indonesia 23 %** (lowest). 🔵 Every other figure this base carries measures **usage**;
this one measures **consent**, and three of four countries moved toward restriction.
🟢 **Delivery pattern in production:** Microsoft × **University of Sydney** runs *educator-designed*
agents — nursing simulations, a 24/7 automotive study agent, academic-writing support, a
sports-marketing mentor. 🔵 That is the competitive shape in APAC HE: the institution's own staff
compose the agents and the vendor supplies the platform.
🔵 **Regulation, carried:** South Korea's **AI Basic Act in force `2026-01-22`**; **Vietnam's Law
on Artificial Intelligence in force `2026-03-01`** (passed `2025-12-10`).
🔴 **Supply signal, and it is blocked:** the region produced the week's largest education artefact —
[`cn-math-taxonomy`](https://github.com/xiaoyang111222/cn-math-taxonomy), 5 483★ and 1 389 forks in
twelve days, aligned to the Chinese national curriculum — and it is **`CC-BY-NC-4.0`**, so `BLOCK`.
🟢 **The engagement consequence of `Trend 69` is concrete:** in APAC K-12 the hardest deliverable is
the **inspectability** that lets a school answer a parent, not the model.

### LATAM

🆕 **`p562` — `2026-10-31`: 🔴 ZERO NEW INSTRUMENTS, two passes after this region out-yielded every other.** The leg returned UNESCO **IESALC × UNU-IAS** (~200 institutions, 19 countries — **87 %** using AI in ≥1 area, **74 %** in teaching and learning, **26 %** with a formal strategy), **Universidad de los Andes 2026**'s **716** AI regulatory instruments across 23 countries (`>80 %` since 2023), the **UNESCO LAC Observatory** (`14 Apr 2026`, Santiago, 11 named partners) and **PISA 2025**'s nine-in-ten across 13 countries — 🔴 **all held**. 🟢 **The governance gap is now carried by two independent measurements and a regulatory-density count, and it has survived four legs**, so it is the safest LATAM sentence this base owns: *adoption is near-universal, formal strategy is not, and the instruments exist in quantity without reaching institutions*. 🔴 **PISA's nine-in-ten reached this pass only through a roundup, not PISA** — carried at existing confidence, **not promoted**.

🆕 **`p561` — `2026-10-31`: 🟢 TWO NEW INSTRUMENTS, AND LATAM OUT-YIELDS THE OTHER THREE REGIONS **2 TO 0** FOR THE THIRD CONSECUTIVE PASS.**
🟢 **New — the governance gap as a procurement fact:** 🔴 **55 % of Latin American and Caribbean universities have no specific AI budget** (UNESCO IESALC 2026, ~200 institutions across 19 countries — the same study that gives this shelf `87 % use at least one AI application` and `26 % have a formal strategy`). 🟢 This is the most directly actionable LATAM number this base holds, because it names the obstruction as **a missing line item** rather than a missing policy: an engagement has to be funded out of digital transformation, accreditation or student-retention budget, or it has nowhere to land. 🔵 The report's own recommendation — public funding for AI governance in higher education — is the counterpart ask.
🟢 **New — reach, on an instrument already held:** Google.org's LAC AI-education programme targets **1.25 million students by 2028** across nine countries, against the **$4.6 M** commitment this base already carries.
🔵 **Confirmed and not new** (seven instruments): Universidad de los Andes' **716 AI-related regulatory instruments across 23 countries**, most begun since 2023; the Digital Education Council's **92 % of students / 79 % of faculty** engaging with AI; Uruguay's **Ceibal at 75 %** of public-school teachers; Brazil's **Cetic.br at 65 %** of internet users aged 9-17 and **53 % of school administrators using AI against 22 % with guidelines**; UNESCO's **LAC Observatory on AI in Education**, launched April 2026 at ECLAC in Santiago; HolonIQ's **2026 Latin America EdTech 100** with Brazil at roughly **69 %** of active regional edtechs and **$2.6 B** of investment; and **Teachy** (Brazil) expanding beyond the region.
🔴 **The LATAM supply blank holds for an eighteenth pass**, and the fragmentation warning is the reason it matters: commentators on the 716-instrument count argue the region is drifting toward **incompatible national rules** and press for common definitions, risk categories and mutual recognition. 🟢 A multi-country LATAM engagement should therefore budget **per-jurisdiction compliance work it cannot amortise** — which is the opposite of the EU single-market assumption, and is where this region's delivery cost actually sits.

🆕 **`p560` — `2026-10-30`: 🟢 THE LATAM THESIS NOW HAS A SECOND, INDEPENDENT MEASUREMENT AT
INSTITUTIONAL SCALE — AND IT IS THE SAME RATIO.** 🟢 **UNESCO IESALC with UNU-IAS**, launched at
Digital Learning Week 2026, surveyed **200 institutions across 19 countries**: 🟢 **87 % use AI in
at least one area**, 🔴 **26 % have a formal AI strategy**, and adoption is driven by individual
academics, researchers and students rather than institutional plans.
🔵 **Why this matters beside `p559`'s figure rather than instead of it.** `p559` carried the
Observatory's *teacher-level* gap — >50 % of teachers in Chile and Brazil using AI tools against
<10 % of institutions with formal guidelines. This is the *institution-level* gap, from a different
body, a different instrument and a different population — and it points the same way at **~3.3:1**.
🟢 **Two independent measurements of one gap is no longer a thesis; it is a finding**, and it says
the LATAM deliverable is the governance layer — policy, procurement, AI literacy in accreditation —
not the pilot.
🟢 **Regulatory surface, quantified:** a **2026 Universidad de los Andes** study counts **716
AI-related regulatory instruments and proposals across 23 countries and territories**, most begun
since 2023. 🔴 Commentators describe the direction as **fragmentation** — 🔵 which is an argument for
designing per-country policy variance into a regional engagement rather than assuming one LATAM
posture.
🟢 **Institutions, carried forward and re-confirmed:** the **UNESCO Regional Observatory on AI in
Education for LAC**, launched **14 April 2026 in Santiago**, with CAF, CENIA, Cetic.br, ECLAC,
Fundación Ceibal, Fundación Santillana, Tecnológico de Monterrey, ProFuturo, Universidad del
Desarrollo and IRCAI.
🔴 **No new LATAM supply this pass.** 🔵 `558-H`'s correction stands and is restated so it is not
re-broken: the standing *"no LATAM-origin supply"* claim is **REFUTED** — 12 addresses, Brazil 8,

🆕 **`p559` — `2026-10-30`: 🟢 TWO NEW LATAM INSTRUMENTS, AND THE REGION OUT-YIELDED EVERY OTHER
THIS PASS.** 🟢 **(1) The UNESCO LAC Observatory's own adoption figures**, which this shelf carried

pending) and its 2025 regulatory sandbox; Chile's executive AI bill in first constitutional stage

[`portabilis/pre-matricula-digital`](https://github.com/portabilis/pre-matricula-digital). 🔴 Both
default to branch **`2.12`**, so both are invisible to a `main`-only licence reader — `559-G`.
🟢 The LATAM opportunity remains **build-on-copyleft-public-sector**, not build-from-nothing.

🟢 **`Gap 549-F`'s comparable field — *what must a product enforce in code here*:**
**an accreditation-grade evidence export.** There is **no binding instrument to comply with**,
which is precisely why the enforcement surface is different: IESALC's recommendation to
governments is to build AI governance **into accreditation and quality assurance**, and the
measured gap is **87 % using against 9 % evaluating**. A product sold here must be able to
emit **usage, outcome and review records a quality-assurance body can audit** — the artefact
that closes a 78-point gap between using and evaluating. 🔵 **The weakest regulatory regime of
the four generates the most demanding reporting requirement**, which is the inversion a
regulation-only reading of this region would miss.

🟢 **The adoption/governance scissors is the region's commercial thesis and it widened

| figure | value | source |
|---|---|---|
| secondary teachers who used AI in the prior year | **Brazil 56 %** · **Chile 55 %** vs **OECD 36 %** | UNESCO |
| regional universities using AI | **87 %** | UNESCO IESALC |
| …of those, with a formal AI strategy | 🔴 **26 %** | UNESCO IESALC |
| Brazilian school administrators using AI | **53 %** | — |
| …whose school has guidelines for it | 🔴 **22 %** | — |

🔵 **Two independent institutional levels — universities and schools — show the same shape:
adoption three to four times governance.** 🟢 **That is a governance-and-enablement
engagement, not a build engagement**, and it is the one LATAM proposition on this base that
does not depend on new open-source supply arriving.

🔵 **Market and policy, carried:** HolonIQ's **2026 LATAM EdTech 100** (Brazil ~half the
cohort; Teachy, Vetto, Mexico's Ginia, Talisis, Mattilda); Brazil's draft AI framework still
under consideration and borrowing the EU's risk-based grammar, plus an EU–Brazil digital
partnership; Chile's government-sponsored risk-based bill; **CENIA: 13 of 19** LAC countries
studied do not teach early AI adoption in schools. 🟢 UNESCO's **regional AI-in-education
observatory** (launched April 2026, with CAF, CENIA, Cetic.br, Ceibal, Fundación Santillana,
Tecnológico de Monterrey, ProFuturo) is the region's convening body and the natural
counterpart for a governance offer.
#### 🆕 `p553` — zero supply for a fourth pass, and the one candidate fails on its own licence

🔴 **The supply blank stands, and it is written down rather than filled.** `p553` found
exactly one LATAM-*placed* addressable artefact,
[`juanmoisesd/ai-educational-impact-latin-america-dataset`](https://github.com/juanmoisesd/ai-educational-impact-latin-america-dataset),
and it fails three tests:

| test | result |
|---|---|
| an implementation? | 🔴 **no** — 54 KB citation shell; the data lives on Mendeley Data, DOI `10.17632/vtc55dyc4n.1`, published `2026-03-03` |
| grant resolved? | 🔴 **no** — `LICENSE` carries **CC0-1.0 abridged** (1 259 B of ~7 000 B), `README` states **CC BY 4.0**, GitHub reads **`NOASSERTION`** — `Gap 553-E` |
| adoption? | 🔴 **0★ / 0 forks**, last pushed `2026-04-08` |

🔴 **And the abridgement bites this region specifically.** The clauses the 1 259 B file omits
include CC0 **s3, the Public License Fallback** — the provision that makes CC0 operate where a
bare copyright waiver is **not recognised in law**, which is the position in most of LATAM's
civil-law jurisdictions. 🔵 The one artefact placed in LATAM is missing the clause that would
make its own dedication work there.

🔴 **Zero new demand claims** — the mandated leg returned nothing this base did not hold. The
LATAM demand leg is blank for a **third** pass, and supply for a **fourth**.

🔵 **The demand picture this base already holds, re-confirmed on this pass's channel:**
teacher AI use from **TALIS 2024** — Brazil **56 %**, Chile **55 %**, Colombia **53 %**,
Costa Rica **52 %**, against an OECD average of **36 %**; **Cetic.br** — **53 %** of school
administrators use AI while only **22 %** report school guidelines; **UNESCO IESALC** —
**87 %** of regional universities use AI, **26 %** have a formal strategy, **9 %** evaluate
implementation.

🟢 **The governance gap *is* the whole opportunity, and it is now triangulated by three
independent instruments** (TALIS, Cetic.br, IESALC) all showing the same shape: adoption far
ahead of policy. 🔵 That is the most durable commercial finding on this page — an AI
**governance and evaluation** engagement is better supported by evidence in LATAM than an AI
**deployment** engagement.

🔵 **Institutional movement carried as context, not as demand:** UNESCO's regional
**Observatory on AI in Education for LAC** launched **14 Apr 2026** (advisory council
including the OECD and the OEI), with a **Mexico pilot from August 2026** co-building centres
of excellence with **CONALEP** and **DGETI**; and **Google.org** committed **$4.6 M** with the
Raspberry Pi Foundation to scale *Experience AI* across nine countries including Brazil and
Mexico, targeting **1.25 M students by 2028**. 🔵 **HolonIQ's 2026 LATAM EdTech 100** records
Brazil as nearly half the cohort, naming **Teachy** (AI lesson preparation, expanded into
Asia) and Mexico's **Ginia** (AI-driven employability). 🔴 **None of these is open source**, so
none becomes a shelf row.


#### 🆕 `p555` — LATAM's supply blank is unchanged, and this pass can finally say it is not an instrument artefact

🔴 **Demand side: no new fact, and the saturation here is total.** UNESCO IESALC/UNU-IAS
(**200** institutions, **19** countries, **87 %** using AI in at least one area, **26 %** with a
formal strategy), Universidad de los Andes' **716** regulatory instruments across 23 countries,
*">80 % initiated since 2023"*, **85 %** of countries with an AI strategy but **only Brazil and

14 April 2026, with CAF, CENIA, Cetic.br, ECLAC, Fundación Ceibal, Tecnológico de Monterrey,
ProFuturo, IRCAI), Google/IDB's **$5M** Google.org funding, **AI Week LATAM 2026** with SoftServe
and NVIDIA — 🔴 **every one of these is already held on this base.**

🔴 **Supply side: still blank.** No new LATAM-origin permissive education repository this pass.
🔵 The LATAM addresses this base carries remain
[`portabilis/i-educar`](https://github.com/portabilis/i-educar) (**722★, GPL-2.0**, Brazil) and
[`academico-sis/academico`](https://github.com/academico-sis/academico), with
`startin-lab/brujula-educativa`, `dsvi-b/corretor-enem`, `LabSirius/TutorIA`, `cortega26/chile-hub`
and `Dramoscuello/simic` in the long tail.

recorded that *"LATAM discovery is instrument-limited, not region-limited — this base cannot
distinguish 'LATAM builds nothing' from 'GitHub search cannot find it'"*. 🟢 **The index's
omission rate is now measured: 5 of 84 queried addresses — 6.0 % — are absent from the search
index while live on `git ls-remote`.** 🔵 **So the blank is real at the order of magnitude that
matters.** A 6 % index blind spot cannot hide a LATAM supply layer; it can hide one or two
repositories. 🔴 `484-G` is therefore **NARROWED, not closed** — its owner-location sweep is
still owed — but the "the index is hiding the region" hypothesis is now **bounded at ~6 %** and
can stop being offered as an explanation for a blank this wide.

🟢 **The engagement this shape actually supports:** LATAM's constraint is **governance capacity**
(87 % using AI, 26 % with a strategy) against **fiscal capacity** (only 2 of 23 countries
budgeting). 🔵 The sellable artefact is an **AI-governance starter kit for a university or
ministry** — policy templates, an accreditation-ready AI-literacy module, and a licence-clearance
gate — built on `i-educar` or Moodle where an institution already runs one. 🔴 **Carried over
unchanged from prior passes, because the demand channel produced nothing to revise it with.**


#### 🆕 `p556` — the supply blank is now proven to be the population, not the instrument

🟢 **The strongest demand evidence on this page, and it is primary.** UNESCO IESALC (with
UNU-IAS) finds **87 % of higher-education institutions in Latin America and the Caribbean using
AI in at least one area of activity**, while *"governance frameworks and institutional
strategies are struggling to keep pace"* and — the operative sentence for a studio — adoption
is *"driven by faculty members, researchers, and students rather than by institutional
policies"*. 🟢 The Digital Education Council's 2026 LATAM survey, **30 000+ responses across 29
institutions** with **Tecnológico de Monterrey**'s Institute for the Future of Education among
the research partners, reports **92 % of students and 79 % of faculty actively engaging with
AI**, and students *"expect clearer guidance and governance"*.

🔵 **Read together those two findings describe a specific, unusual market: adoption is already
near-universal and bottom-up, and the missing layer is institutional governance.** That is not a
build engagement. It is policy, guardrails, assessment-integrity design and staff enablement on
top of tools the faculty already chose — and it is available *now*, with no platform
procurement in front of it.

🔴 **Regulation is the slow leg, and no country has an in-force general AI law reaching
education.** Brazil's **PL 2338/2023** passed the Senate in December 2024 and remains pending in
the Câmara dos Deputados. Chile's AI bill has a last recorded milestone of **`2026-01-20`**,
with the Ministry of Education's 2025 teacher guidance cited in the Senate committee's own
document. Mexico has no general law; the **Estado de México amended art. 61 of its education
law in April 2026** to promote responsible AI use in upper-secondary and higher education — a
**state** instrument, not federal — and SEP and ANUIES guidance is explicitly non-binding.
🔵 A regional index across 20 economies reports **85 % of countries with an AI strategy in place
or under development, and only Brazil and Colombia allocating budget** within those instruments.
🟢 **Strategy without budget is the regional signature**, and it is why the governance work is
bought by institutions rather than by ministries here.

🔴 **The supply blank, probed in the region's own languages for the first time.** `p553`–`p555`
recorded LATAM as a supply blank without testing it in Spanish or Portuguese. 🟢 This pass ran
`educación OR educativo OR educação OR docentes tutor IA in:name,description,readme stars:>20`,
sorted by stars: **73 results, zero LATAM education artefacts.** The nearest misses are both
instructive and neither qualifies —
[`DragonJAR/n8n-workflows-esp`](https://github.com/DragonJAR/n8n-workflows-esp) (527★,
Spanish-language n8n workflows: 🔵 LATAM-origin, automation not education) and
[`cppbrasil/material-de-aprendizado`](https://github.com/cppbrasil/material-de-aprendizado)
(441★, Brazilian C/C++ study materials: 🔵 LATAM-origin and educational, but content not AI).
🟢 **Verdict: the blank is a property of the population.** The region's one shelved artefact
stays [`GuilhermeScarpini/agente-bncc-professores`](https://github.com/GuilhermeScarpini/agente-bncc-professores)
(MIT, Brazil, BNCC/MEC-grounded, 🔴 **0★**).

🟢 **Which makes the LATAM opportunity the clearest on this page: world-leading adoption,
near-zero published open-source supply, and a permissive offline-first platform that fits the
region's connectivity profile exactly** — [`learningequality/kolibri`](https://github.com/learningequality/kolibri)
(1 162★, 🟢 **MIT**, offline-first, SDG-4/OER) — plus
[`chamilo/chamilo-lms`](https://github.com/chamilo/chamilo-lms) (1 019★, 🟡 GPL-3.0) and
[`francoisjacquet/rosariosis`](https://github.com/francoisjacquet/rosariosis) (649★, 🟡 GPL-2.0,
Spanish-language SIS) as the self-host options where an institution already runs them.

the blanks were a transport artefact, not an absence** (`557-F`): the demand host is

learning** — and **only 26 % have a formal AI strategy**. 🔵 Adoption is bottom-up, driven by

engagement in LATAM HE is governance and strategy, not tooling** — the tooling is already in the

🟢 **And there is now a reference deployment at scale:** a **Chilean university running 194

on this base that is countable rather than aspirational.

🆕 **`p562` — `2026-10-31`: 🟢 THE GLOBAL LEGS PAID ON SUPPLY, NOT DEMAND — and the finding is a licence-tooling one with a number attached.** 🔴 **38 of 91** Apache-2.0/AGPL-3.0 rows this base sells are **canonically verbatim while a raw-byte licence gate reports them modified** — `41.8 %` (`561-F`, closed; census on [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md#-p562--2026-10-31)). 🟢 **That is a global delivery risk, not a regional one:** every engagement stands a licence gate up in week one, and four in ten of the components a proposal recommends will fail it for formatting. 🟢 The remedy is shipped as a pattern this pass — `P562-A` on [`../compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md#-p562--2026-10-31) — wired from four Apache-2.0 scanners already on this shelf plus the normalising comparator. 🔵 On market size nothing moved: the `$10.6 B → $42.48 B (2030)` band and `559-D`'s **5.9×** base-year spread both return unchanged, and `557-D`'s refusal to quote a single total stands.

🆕 **`p561` — `2026-10-31`: 🔵 THE MARKET LEDGER IS UNCHANGED AND THE LEG CONFIRMS IT RATHER THAN WIDENING IT.** This pass's global sizing leg returned **$9.58 B** (Precedence Research, 2026 base, self-declared boundary-dependent), **$10.6 B → $42.48 B by 2030 at 41.5 % CAGR** (Research and Markets), **$11.4 B → $57.2 B by 2033 at 25.9 % CAGR** (Grand View Research) and **$8.9 B → $73.3 B by 2033 at 35.10 % CAGR** (Market.us) — 🟢 **all four already in this ledger with their scope words recorded** (`559-D`). 🔴 The 2026 base spread stays **`$1.94 B → $11.4 B` = 5.9×** and 🔴 **no single value is adopted** (`558-F`).
🔵 **One relay added a fifth framing and it was refused:** Technavio's **45 % CAGR** is reported against an *absolute increase* (`USD 3 367.8 M` of growth, 2026-2030), not a total — 🔴 so it is not commensurable with the others and is not entered. 🟢 Exactly the scope-word discipline `559-D` installed, exercised on a new firm.
🟢 **The global supply picture gained a structural fact this pass, from `p561`'s grading rather than from a market report.** Across 25 shelf rows re-read first-hand: 🟢 **the permissive layer is larger than the byte figures suggested** — four rows whose raw digests match nothing canonical are canonical **verbatim** (`561-F`), and 🔴 **the one row with a genuinely modified operative grant is the platform layer, not the agent layer** ([`openedx/openedx-platform`](https://github.com/openedx/openedx-platform), `561-B`). 🔵 For a studio deciding where to put its legal review hours, that inverts the obvious allocation: the agents are mostly clean, and **the LMS underneath them is where the clause is**.

application category** — MarketReportsWorld ~$1.94 B) sits at the bottom. 🔵 `559-D` asked for the
scope word *"so the spread can be decomposed instead of merely declared"*. 🟢 **Decomposed: the
spread tracks scope breadth, and it is therefore not an error bar at all.**

any deck, summary or pattern — a midpoint across incompatible boundaries measures nothing that
exists, and the new low outlier moves that midpoint without adding information. 🟢 **What survives
and is safe to quote:** segment **rank order**, stable across every firm that breaks out segments —
**K-12 first at ~34 %**.

studies** reports the field fragmented, with pedagogical design still poorly connected to technical

🟢 **Added at `p554`, and it is content rather than a shim: this block was missing a
`Global` subsection, and `gate-region-headings` had been RED on exactly that since the
