### 🔴 The one thing that *did* move, and it is a supply finding that crosses regions

🟢 **`p543` established that the non-English supply side has a licence problem
and called it regional** — three Vietnamese education agents, zero licences.
🔴 **`p544` found a fourth, in an unrelated region, with the same result:**
[`fairyofmiracle/edu_course_agent`](https://github.com/fairyofmiracle/edu_course_agent),
**EMEA (Russia)**, real, live, education-specific, **ungranted**.

🔴 **So the licence problem is not regional — it is a property of
domestic, non-English-corpus supply wherever it occurs.** Four artefacts, two
unrelated origins (APAC/Vietnam and EMEA/Russia), **zero grants**. 🟢 The class now
has a published register:
[`measured-unusable.md`](measured-unusable.md).

🔵 **The commercial consequence, stated plainly because it changes what may be
promised in a pitch:** when a client in one of these markets asks what local
open-source foundations exist, the honest answer is **"they exist and you cannot
build on them."** 🔴 **Counting artefacts without reading grants overstates every
regional supply claim on this page.** 🟢 The cheapest corrective action is also
the one most likely to work: **ask for a licence.** A single `LICENSE` commit
moves a row from unusable to shelf-eligible, and `ACTION p417-A` already
prescribes exactly this for a LATAM address.

🔴 **A methodological warning this pass earned, and it applies to every regional
claim on this page.** `p544` proved that **the language of a query does not imply
the origin of what it returns** — a Ukrainian-language leg returned a Russian
artefact. 🔵 **Any region on this base that was attributed from the leg that found
it, rather than read from the artefact or the source, is unproven.** Registered as
`Gap 544-A`.

### Opportunities by region — `p544` (historical; the authoritative block is the single `##` at the top of this page)

### North America

🆕 **`p545` — demand leg: saturated, nothing new.** Re-run in the mandated form, the leg
returned **Idaho SB 1227** (statewide K-12 framework, AI-literacy standards, local
policies, and a bar on AI *replacing* a teacher), **Maryland**'s 120-day deadline for
aligned district policies, **NYC**'s March-2026 preliminary guidance with a June playbook
and a rule that **student data may not be used to train models**, the **SUNY** systemwide
policy putting AI literacy into general education for undergraduates entering **autumn
2026**, and the **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026**, introduced in April and
**still not passed**. 🔵 **All already held.** 🔴 **One figure is explicitly NOT adopted:**
a tracker's *"more than 1 500 AI-related bills introduced nationwide this year"* counts
**all** AI bills, not education bills, and so cannot be compared with this page's retired
*"134 bills / 31 states"* — **different quantity, not a correction of it.**

#### 🆕 `p544` — the NA leg restated for a third pass, so the opportunity is **sequencing**, not discovery

🔴 **Nothing new was found, and the engagement implication is not "nothing to
do."** The NA regulatory picture on this page is now **stable across three
passes** — the four dual-mandate states, `A 9190`'s ninth-grade floor, the K-12
AI Literacy and Readiness Act, STUDENTS FIRST. 🟢 **A stable picture is what makes
a fixed-scope offer possible**: the governance artefacts are known, so the
sellable work is **implementing** them rather than researching them.

🟢 **The concrete asymmetry to sell against, from this page's own numbers:**
Gallup puts **60 %** of US teachers using AI while **55 %** of high-school
principals have not restricted access and most districts still have no formal
student policy. 🔵 **Usage is ahead of governance by a wide margin, and the gap is
measured rather than asserted.** The DC model — barring AI from grading,
discipline, IEP decisions and teacher evaluation — is a **drafted, citable
boundary set** a district engagement can adopt in week one instead of
commissioning.


#### 🆕 `p543` — two governance clauses promoted out of history, and they are the sellable artefacts

🔴 **Neither of these was new to this base; both were trending-only until this
pass** (`Gap 529-A`). 🟢 **Both are concrete enough to reuse in a policy
engagement**, which is why they belong on a curated page:

- **Berkeley Law** bans AI on **all exams and credited coursework** — a
  blanket-prohibition template at a top-tier professional school.
- **Chicago's** board-approved district policy bars AI as the **sole basis for
  a high-stakes decision** — a *decision-rights* template rather than a
  usage-ban template.

🔵 **Those are the two poles of the North American policy space**, and an
institution asking "what should our policy say" is really asking which pole it
sits nearer. 🟢 **The opportunity is the instrument, not the model**: a
decision-rights policy needs an audit trail per decision, which is an
engineering deliverable; a usage ban needs assessment redesign, which is not.

**🆕 `p541` — the automated-grading slot is now permissive, which changes what a
district pilot can be.** `P584` (policy conformance) and every assessment
pattern on this base previously had to route through copyleft graders. This
pass filed three **MIT** graders whose licences were hidden one rung along the
ladder — [`rstudio/ggcheck`](https://github.com/rstudio/ggcheck) and
[`rstudio/tblcheck`](https://github.com/rstudio/tblcheck) for statistics
coursework, [`shmercer/writeAlizer`](https://github.com/shmercer/writeAlizer)
for **student writing quality** — plus **BSD-3-Clause**
[`OpenOLAT/qtiworks`](https://github.com/OpenOLAT/qtiworks) for QTI 2.1
delivery. 🟢 **For a US district or state engagement this is the difference
between a grading pilot a vendor can ship and one that obliges source
release.** 🔴 The regional policy picture is unchanged from `p540` and is not
restated here; see the note at the head of this file.

**🆕 `p540` — policy-conformance tooling, because 134 bills across 31 states
produced policies nobody can enforce.** The 2026 session left districts with
written AI policies (Maryland: 120 days from MSDE guidance; Oklahoma: before
2027-28) and **no instrumentation to show compliance**. Concretely: an
[`ucfopen/canvasapi`](https://github.com/ucfopen/canvasapi) (MIT) reader over a
Canvas tenant, emitting xAPI through
[`yetanalytics/xapipe`](https://github.com/yetanalytics/xapipe) (Apache-2.0),
producing a per-course record of where AI touched a grade — which is exactly
what Oklahoma's *"AI may not be the primary basis for grading, discipline or
placement"* provision requires a district to be able to demonstrate. 🟢 The
ADA web-accessibility update (Apr 2026) is the second surface, and the one AI
demonstrably helps with. 🔴 **Canada remains a blank on this leg and should be
searched by name, not folded into North America.**

🆕 **`p539` — the US regulatory surface is now countable, and it is state-level.**
One tracker counts **134 AI-in-education bills introduced across 31 states in
2026**. There is still **no federal education-AI statute**; federal oversight
runs through executive action. 🔵 **For a studio this is the defining structural
fact of the region: there is no single compliance target, so the sellable
artefact is a policy-configurable system, not a compliant one.** Named
instruments read this pass:

| Instrument | What it does | Status as read |
|---|---|---|
| **California AB 1159** | student data and the training of AI models on it | bill |
| **Maryland — AI Ready Schools** | state education department issues AI guidance; districts designate **AI coordinators** | programme |
| **Oklahoma S.B. 1734** | every district must adopt a **written AI policy** before 2027–28 | bill |
| **South Carolina H.B. 5253** | 🔴 the strictest read this pass — **parental opt-in consent**, limits on AI replacing licensed teachers | bill |
| **Utah H.B. 273** | folds AI into state **computer-science standards** | enacted per source |
| **Georgia, Mississippi** | AI-related CS credit requirements | per source |
| **New York City** | preliminary guidance on a **traffic-light** framework that **bars AI from grading, discipline and placement decisions** | district guidance |
| **Ohio** | districts required to adopt AI-use policies by **1 Jul 2026** | in force |
| **SUNY** | systemwide AI policy approved spring 2026; **AI literacy in general education** for incoming students from fall 2026 | in force |

🔴 **The NYC line is the one that kills a product.** A traffic-light framework
that bars AI from grading, discipline and placement removes the three highest-value
automation targets in a K-12 pitch. 🟢 **What survives it, and what this base can
now point at:** drafting, differentiation, accessibility and administrative load —
and the region's new supply rows serve exactly those.
[`AnubisLMS/Anubis`](https://github.com/AnubisLMS/Anubis) (MIT, 382★, NYU Tandon)
autogrades **code by test execution**, which is a deterministic check rather than
an AI judgement and sits outside the ban;
[`Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Ed-Fi-ODS`](https://github.com/Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Ed-Fi-ODS)
(Apache-2.0) is the records substrate a district already runs.
🟡 **Canada returned nothing again** — fourth pass with no Canadian result from
this leg. Recorded as a gap, not as coverage.

> 🆕 **`p538` — supply placed here for the first time, and it answers this
> section's own demand findings.** Everything above is US regulation: 134 bills,
> FERPA, COPPA's amended rule, state disclosure statutes. 🔴 **This base has
> catalogued that demand for many passes with no permissive supply to point an
> engagement at.** Three MIT repositories now exist and they compose into one
> compliance spine:
> [`state-ai-disclosure-state-tracker`](https://github.com/mizcausevic-dev/state-ai-disclosure-state-tracker)
> (hash-chained per-state lifecycle record; names Illinois SOPPA, California
> AB 1584 / AB 2876, Texas HB 18, NY Ed Law 2-d),
> [`pii-student-vault-contract-profile`](https://github.com/mizcausevic-dev/pii-student-vault-contract-profile)
> (**FERPA's 7 PII categories** and **COPPA's 10 PI categories** mapped to vault
> targets, with retention, redaction and a four-doctrine consent taxonomy) and
> [`student-data-access-audit-stream`](https://github.com/mizcausevic-dev/student-data-access-audit-stream)
> (tamper-evident **FERPA §99.32** access log with a Node verifier). 🔴 **All
> three are 0–1★ v0.1 drafts by one author with no adoption**, and each says of
> itself *"EdTech-readiness scaffolding, not certification."* 🔵 **So the
> commercial read is: use them as a specification, bill the implementation.**
> The category mappings and the §99.32 rule are what an engagement would
> otherwise derive from statute by hand, and that derivation is the expensive
> part. 🟢 Also newly placed here:
> [`dougpenny/SwiftyPSCore`](https://github.com/dougpenny/SwiftyPSCore) (MIT,
> 6★) — the first permissively granted client for **PowerSchool**, the dominant
> US K-12 SIS. 🔴 Swift-only, so an agent runtime cannot link it directly.


> 🆕 **`p536` — the opportunity is *integration into an institutional stack that
> already exists*, and this pass found three layers of it unowned.** 🟢 **Learning
> Commons** publishes the rubric layer ([`evaluators`](https://github.com/learning-commons-org/evaluators),
> 87★) and the instructional-content layer
> ([`knowledge-graph`](https://github.com/learning-commons-org/knowledge-graph), 164★)
> that Anthropic's `k12-teacher-skills` is built against — so a US K-12 engagement can
> be **grounded in audited instructional content with published rubrics on day one**
> rather than in a prompt. 🔴 **The billable complication is the grant, and it is
> real:** the datasets license **per download** across Open / Open + Gated / Gated, and
> the payload states that distribution *"doesn't consolidate those into a single license
> for your build."* 🟢 **That is a service, not an obstacle** — `P572` is the
> access-type-aware ingest that makes a build shippable, and the client cannot do it
> themselves because it requires reading the catalogue per release.
> 🟢 **Second opening: efficacy as a deliverable.** With
> [`terracotta`](https://github.com/terracotta-education/terracotta) (Apache-2.0,
> randomisation + consent + de-identification inside the LMS) plus `dowhy` / `EconML`
> from `p535`, a studio can now sell **an effect estimate**, not a pilot — against a
> buyer side where **~10 % of institutions have formal AI guidelines** and only ~20 of
> 800+ K-12 AI studies produced strong causal evidence. 🔵 **Third, and cheapest:** MyLA
> ([`my-learning-analytics`](https://github.com/tl-its-umich-edu/my-learning-analytics),
> Apache-2.0, 39 forks against 37 stars) is a peer university's production dashboard —
> `P569`'s "start from a peer institution's code" play, with a second instance.


> 🆕 **`p534` — this is the pass's strongest supply leg, and all four finds are
> institutional 0★–5★ repositories, not products.** The engagement opening is
> specific: **~10 % of North American institutions have formal AI guidelines and
> 71 % of US teachers have no AI training**, against a sector with *no regulator*
> and a state-by-state patchwork. 🟢 **What is newly buildable:** MIT Open
> Learning's own stack is permissive end to end — [`learn-ai`](https://github.com/mitodl/learn-ai)
> (**MIT**, four LangGraph agents, RAG eval, Opik/LangSmith/PostHog tracing) on
> [`agent-kit`](https://github.com/mitodl/agent-kit) (**BSD-3-Clause**) — so a
> university engagement can start from a peer institution's production code
> rather than a vendor demo. 🔵 And the **Ed-Fi** data plane is the other half:
> two Apache-2.0 repositories from the standards body US K-12 student records
> move on, which is the governance artefact the 10 %-with-guidelines number says
> nobody has. 🔴 Regional sizing disagrees with the global band by an order of
> magnitude — $951M (2024) → $2 303.2M (2029) at 15.9 % for North America, on a
> $3.7–10.6B global base — so **do not mix the two sources in one slide.**

> 🆕 **`p533` — supply added on this leg.** [`autolab/Autolab`](https://github.com/autolab/Autolab)
> (**Apache-2.0**, 808★, **Carnegie Mellon**) is the permissive autograding
> course-management platform, the North American counterpart to Artemis, and this
> base had no autograder row at all before this pass.
> [`uPortal-Project/uPortal`](https://github.com/uPortal-Project/uPortal)
> (**Apache-2.0**, 287★, Apereo) is the higher-ed student portal — the
> authenticated front door an assistant belongs inside.
> [`Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Data-Management-Service`](https://github.com/Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Data-Management-Service)
> (**Apache-2.0**) is the successor generation of the region's K-12 data
> interoperability layer. And [`freeCodeCamp/freeCodeCamp`](https://github.com/freeCodeCamp/freeCodeCamp)
> (**BSD-3-Clause**, **458,181★**) is a reusable *curriculum*, which is the one
> asset class this base has never had on any leg. All on
> [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md).

> 🆕 **`p531` — supply placed.** The region's permissive education supply in the
> drop set is **assessment plumbing and compliance scaffolding, not tutors**:
> GitHub's own autograders
> ([`classroom-resources/autograding-python-grader`](https://github.com/classroom-resources/autograding-python-grader),
> MIT, 11★;
> [`autograding-io-grader`](https://github.com/classroom-resources/autograding-io-grader),
> MIT, 7★), [`edulinq/autograder-py`](https://github.com/edulinq/autograder-py)
> (MIT, 16★, 21 forks), the **Ed-Fi** deployment tooling
> ([`Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Ed-Fi-AdminApp`](https://github.com/Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Ed-Fi-AdminApp),
> [`xmarcosx/edfi-via-kubernetes`](https://github.com/xmarcosx/edfi-via-kubernetes)),
> the PowerSchool client libraries
> ([`dougpenny/SwiftyPSCore`](https://github.com/dougpenny/SwiftyPSCore),
> [`IronCountySchoolDistrict/powerschool-alchemy`](https://github.com/IronCountySchoolDistrict/powerschool-alchemy)),
> and a **FERPA/COPPA readiness suite**
> ([`mizcausevic-dev/state-ai-disclosure-state-tracker`](https://github.com/mizcausevic-dev/state-ai-disclosure-state-tracker),
> [`student-data-access-audit-stream`](https://github.com/mizcausevic-dev/student-data-access-audit-stream),
> [`pii-student-vault-contract-profile`](https://github.com/mizcausevic-dev/pii-student-vault-contract-profile))
> that names IL SOPPA, CA AB 1584/AB 2876, TX HB 18, NY Ed Law 2-d, VA ChAIPA
> and CO SB 196 as concrete obligations. 🔵 **The opportunity this implies:** in
> North America the differentiator is not the tutor, it is **being able to
> answer the procurement questionnaire** — and the artefacts for that are
> permissive and already written, at 0–1★, by people who read the statutes.
> 🔴 **Verify them before use at that star count**; the value is the obligation
> mapping, not the code.

**Regulatory posture.** State-led and moving fast: **134+ bills across 31 states**
on AI in K-12 and higher education (MultiState and FutureEd trackers), mostly on
data privacy, classroom-use limits and curriculum. Named 2026 statutes:

| Jurisdiction | Instrument | What it requires |
|---|---|---|
| **Maryland** | AI Ready Schools Act | All **24** local districts must adopt aligned AI policies within **120 days** of MSDE guidance |
| **Oklahoma** | S.B. 1734 | Written district AI policy **before the 2027-28 school year**; **bans AI as the main basis for high-stakes decisions** |
| 🆕 **Idaho** | S.B. 1227 | Student **data privacy** for AI tools in schools |
| 🆕 **Utah** | H.B. 218 | Required **grade 7/8 digital-skills course including AI literacy** |
| **Virginia** | H.B. 1186 | AI Innovation in Education Pilot Program |
| **Federal** | **H.R. 8747**, K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026 | Advanced out of House Education & Workforce Committee on **21 July**, largely along party lines; would let schools spend federal funds on AI curriculum |

**Players.** Instructure (Canvas) dominates higher-ed LMS; the NEA and AFT have
issued AI guidance (and drawn criticism for it from Defending Education); a
student-authored national framework from all 50 states, published via Day of AI,
rejects both an outright ban and unrestricted adoption.

**Opportunities.**
1. **District policy-compliance layer.** Five states now mandate district-level
   AI policy or curriculum and one forbids AI-determined high-stakes decisions. A
   deployment that *cannot technically* let a model decide a grade —
   human-in-the-loop enforced in code, with an audit trail — is a procurement
   requirement, not a feature. Build the acceptability yardstick on
   [anthropics/k12-teacher-skills](https://github.com/anthropics/k12-teacher-skills)
   eval rubrics (Apache-2.0, 601★) and the model-side yardstick on
   [pedagogy-benchmark](https://github.com/AI-for-Education/pedagogy-benchmark)
   (MIT) — CDPK and SEND answer *"does this model know how to teach"*, which is
   the question an Oklahoma-compliant procurement actually asks.
2. **Canvas-attached agents.** Canvas is AGPL-3.0 and must not be forked;
   [vishalsachdev/canvas-mcp](https://github.com/vishalsachdev/canvas-mcp) (MIT,
   308★, v1.16.0 Oct 2026) is a ready, licence-clean seam into the dominant North
   American LMS — and the most mature LMS-MCP bridge in the sector for any
   platform.
3. **Teacher time recovery, measured.** 5.9 hours/week is a defensible ROI number
   for a lesson-prep and feedback agent — and it is measurable, which makes it
   sellable to a district CFO rather than a CIO.
   🆕 [`SirhanMacx/Claw-ED`](https://github.com/SirhanMacx/Claw-ED) (MIT, 63★) is
   a working, honestly-scoped reference for exactly this shape: local-first,
   single-teacher, lesson drafts from the teacher's own curriculum, teacher review
   before use. Its explicit *"not multi-tenant"* limit is the gap a studio fills.

#### 🆕 `p529` — North America: the governance deficit, quantified

- 🔴 **Only ~10% of institutions have formal AI guidelines**, and **71% of US
  teachers report no AI training** (trade roundup; methodology not published —
  treat as indicative, but note it points the same way as Gallup's 60% already
  on this file).
- **Market:** USD **951M (2024) → ~USD 2.3B (2029)**, ~15.9% CAGR; **~36% of
  global share**, the largest single region (MarketsandMarkets).
- **Canada:** the proposed federal **Artificial Intelligence and Data Act
  (AIDA)** is described as targeting risk mitigation and transparency for
  *high-impact* systems — ⚠️ **not education-specific**, which is the fourth
  consecutive pass the Canada leg has returned workplace rather than education
  policy.
- **Sub-federal:** commentary names **Colorado** and **Texas** as having
  introduced piecemeal requirements, against a federal picture one source calls
  a *"regulatory vacuum"* for education AI with **no FDA-equivalent** for EdTech.
- 🔵 **The opportunity this prices.** NA is the largest market, ships **zero** of
  the per-university LMS bridges counted in
  [`../repos/trending.md`](../repos/trending.md), and has a **90% institutional
  policy gap**. That combination — buys rather than builds, and cannot yet say
  what "acceptable" means — is why `k12-teacher-skills`' **eval rubrics** are the
  most commercially loaded artefact on the shelf: a procurement conversation here
  starts with *"prove it teaches acceptably"*, not *"prove it works"*.


#### 🆕 `p530` — the adoption/governance gap is now two-sourced, and faculty intent is *falling*

| Finding | Source | Figure |
|---|---|---|
| Staff who used AI for work in the last six months | EDUCAUSE, n = **1 960** higher-ed professionals | 🟢 **94%** |
| …who are aware of **any** governing policy | same survey | 🔴 **54%** |
| Faculty **intent to use** AI, US + Canada | Digital Education Council 2026 | 🔴 **76% → 67%**, a 9-point fall |
| …same measure outside US + Canada | same survey | largely unchanged |
| Students who think instructors are equipped to guide AI use | same survey | 🔴 **29%** |
| K-12 principals given any AI policy or guidance | RAND 2025 | 🔴 **45%** |

🔴 **The one genuinely new signal: North American faculty intent is the only
regional figure that moved, and it moved down.** Every other region held flat.
🔵 **Read it as a sales signal, not a demand signal.** A 9-point fall against
94% actual use is not retreat from AI; it is retreat from *unmanaged* AI — the
gap between "I use it" and "my institution has told me how" is **40 points**.
🟢 **What sells into that is governance, evaluation and audit trails, not another
tutor.** The permissive components for exactly that are now on
[`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md) (xAPI LRS, autograders) and
composed in **P559**/**P560**.

🟡 **Source quality, stated.** EDUCAUSE and Digital Education Council are the two
credible instruments here. Aggregator figures in the same search ranged from
*"86% of students"* to *"92% of university students"* with no method given, and
one vendor page asserted a 71% adaptive-platform forecast with no source. **Not
recorded.**


#### 🆕 `p542` — **FIPSE** named, and a consortium-placed artefact

- 🟢 **The `$169 M` this base has carried since `P515` has a vehicle: FIPSE**
  (the US Department of Education's Fund for the Improvement of Postsecondary
  Education), reported as awards including support for responsible AI use.
  **`unverified-at-primary`** — attributed to a market newsletter, and the
  reachable sources do not name the programme consistently. 🔵 **Why it is
  worth filing anyway:** a bare dollar figure cannot be bid against, while a
  **named award vehicle can be looked up, has eligibility rules and has a
  cycle.** This is the difference between a slide number and a pipeline entry,
  and the next pass should read the FIPSE award list directly.
- 🟢 **A named consortium, not an inferred country.** `1111philo/learn`
  (AGPL-3.0, archived 2026-04-17) was built by the 11:11 Philosopher's Group
  with **UIC Tech Solutions, the UIC TS Open Source Fund, Louisiana Tech and
  the ULL Louisiana Educate Program** — public-university technology funds
  commissioning agentic courseware. 🔵 **The artefact is archived and the
  pattern is the finding:** state-university open-source funds are a buyer and
  a co-developer in this region, and they publish under **AGPL**, which shapes
  any derivative engagement before it starts.
- 🔴 **Opportunity, stated against the constraint:** the strongest NA shape
  remains integration into incumbent LMS/SIS estates, and the newly shelved
  [`DaRL-GenAI/instructional_agents`](https://github.com/DaRL-GenAI/instructional_agents)
  (MIT, EACL 2026) is the first permissive asset on this base that addresses
  **faculty workload**, which is what the `$169 M`-class funding actually buys.

### EMEA

🆕 **`p545` — demand leg: saturated, and the deferral is confirmed from a second
direction.** The leg returned the **Digital Omnibus on AI, Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**,
moving Annex III high-risk duties for stand-alone education systems from **2 Aug 2026** to
**2 Dec 2027** — a **16-month** extension — while **Article 50** transparency (telling a
learner they are talking to an AI) has applied since **2 Aug 2026**, the
**emotion-recognition ban in schools** since **February 2025**, and **Article 4** AI
literacy throughout. 🔵 **All already held.** 🟢 **The one thing worth re-stating for an
engagement:** admissions, assessment, placement and proctoring are the Annex III
categories, so **the deferral buys build time, not an exemption** — and a tool that never
leaves the teacher's machine (the posture `capy-note` takes) moves the processing question
rather than deferring it.

#### 🆕 `p544` — EMEA: the demand blank reaches **six passes**, and the supply side just gained an unusable row

🔴 **Stated plainly so silence is not read as coverage: the EMEA *demand* leg is a
measured blank for the sixth consecutive pass.** Queried for adoption and players
it returned **US-only sampling frames**. 🔵 **There is still no EMEA education-AI
market-size or vendor-share study on this base**, and that is a real gap in the
record, not a gap in the region.

🟢 **What EMEA does have is the clearest regulatory calendar of any region, and it
is an engagement driver on its own:** Article 50 transparency duties are **already
in force** (`2026-08-02`) while stand-alone Annex III high-risk obligations do not
bite until **`2027-12-02`**. 🔵 **That 16-month split is the whole sequencing
argument** — a chatbot-disclosure and AI-literacy workstream is due *now*, and the
conformity-assessment work for assessment and admissions systems can be staged.

🔴 **And EMEA is now where the ungranted-supply class turned up outside Asia.**
[`fairyofmiracle/edu_course_agent`](https://github.com/fairyofmiracle/edu_course_agent)
(Russia) is real, education-specific and carries **no licence** →
[`measured-unusable.md`](measured-unusable.md). 🔵 Its subject matter is
instructive regardless of its licence: it automates **mandatory
professional-development paperwork**, and compliance-document generation is a
demand signal this page has not otherwise captured for the region.


#### 🆕 `p543` — Türkiye enters this page with licensed, institutionally-anchored supply

🟢 **Two MIT assets, both anchored to a named national institution**, which is
the pattern `p543` measured as the actual predictor of language-leg yield:

- [`kmkarakaya/btk_rag_bot_egitimi`](https://github.com/kmkarakaya/btk_rag_bot_egitimi)
  — **MIT**, a course corpus for **BTK**, Türkiye's ICT authority: course 514,
  9 h 22 m, 55 lessons, Colab + Gemini + Gradio.
- [`captainmgc/tubitak-skills`](https://github.com/captainmgc/tubitak-skills)
  — **MIT**, agent skills for a **TÜBİTAK 2209-A** research proposal, producing
  `docx`/`pdf` in the funder's own template.

🔵 **And a third asset that is the more transferable observation:**
[`komunite/ai-engineering`](https://github.com/komunite/ai-engineering) is a
**declared, maintained Turkish fork** (1 147 commits) of a held English
curriculum. 🟢 **That is the shape an EMEA capability-building engagement
takes** — the pedagogy is upstream, and **the language and institutional
framing are the local work.** It is cheaper than authoring and it is what the
market is visibly already doing.

🟡 **The regulatory anchor, promoted out of history this pass:** the **EU AI
Office** and national authorities began enforcing the AI Act on
**2026-08-02**, while `p380`'s **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** (OJ 2026-07-24)
defers the Annex III high-risk obligations — which cover proctoring,
admissions and automated assessment — to **2 December 2027**. 🔴 **Both dates
are attributed to secondary sources and marked unverified at primary**, because
`education.ec.europa.eu` does not resolve from this session.

**🆕 `p541` — EMEA stops being this file's supply-poor region, and the reason is
that its supply is state-owned.** The EMEA leg was a measured blank on
*demand* at `p531` and the policy leg is saturated. The **supply** leg,
re-read this pass, returned the largest block of **state-operated** education
software on this base: Finland's National Agency for Education publishes
**seven national registries** — curriculum, learner identity, study records,
admissions, institution register, vocational plans and state funding — under
**EUPL**, filed in
[`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md#-emea-public-sector--opetushallitus-seven-national-registries-under-eupl)
and placed as a platform layer in
[`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md). 🔴 **EUPL is
reciprocal and six of the seven are version 1.1, not 1.2** — so these are
integration targets and data models, not components. 🟢 **The engagement shape
that unlocks is a public-sector one**: an EMEA ministry or agency buyer gets a
working, legally published national schema set to integrate against instead of
a greenfield data model. 🟢 Spain also supplied the pass's strongest small
agent — [`cjescudero/asistente-docente`](https://github.com/cjescudero/asistente-docente),
**MIT**, twelve teacher skills that exchange **Moodle GIFT** question banks.

**🆕 `p540` — the two duties already in force outrank the 2027 one.** The EU
**AI-literacy duty** and the **ban on emotion recognition in education** have
applied since **February 2025**; Annex III high-risk obligations move to
**December 2027** (*Digital Omnibus*, Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 — two sources,
both secondary, dating still unresolved). 🔵 **Schools are deployers and remain
responsible for third-party tools**, so the near-term engagement is a
deployer-side AI inventory plus a literacy programme the Commission/OECD joint
framework of **18 June 2026** now gives a reference shape for — not a
conformity assessment nobody owes until 2027. 🔴 **Still no EMEA market-size or
vendor-share study on this base**, so size the opportunity from institution
counts, not from a market figure.

🆕 **`p539` — EMEA's supply gap was *maturity*, and three state-backed rows now
answer it.** `p538` closed the EMEA/APAC count imbalance with two 1★
repositories by one author and said plainly that the region still had **no
education agent in three figures of stars**. The count is still the weak part,
but the *institutional* half changed this pass:

| Row | Grant | Read | Who is behind it |
|---|---|---|---|
| [`oaknational/oak-open-curriculum-ecosystem`](https://github.com/oaknational/oak-open-curriculum-ecosystem) | 🟢 MIT | 8★ · 5 forks · **6 835 commits** | **Oak National Academy** + **Education Endowment Foundation**, against the **National Curriculum for England (2014)** — API, SDK **and MCP server** over national curriculum data |
| [`DFE-Digital/teaching-vacancies`](https://github.com/DFE-Digital/teaching-vacancies) | 🟢 MIT | 27★ · 23 forks · **19 739 commits** | the **Department for Education**, England — a live national service that also publishes *"Guidance for AI coding agents"* |
| [`KonstantinosPetrakis/esco-skill-extractor`](https://github.com/KonstantinosPetrakis/esco-skill-extractor) | 🟢 MIT | 32★ · 13 forks | the **SKILLAB EU Horizon** project, against the EU's own **ESCO/ISCO** taxonomy |

🟢 **So the EMEA engagement opening is no longer "find a partner" — it is
"extend a published national asset."** An England curriculum engagement starts
from Oak's ontology and MCP server; an EU skills or credential engagement starts
from ESCO codes. Both are MIT.

🔵 **And the regulatory posture rewards a specific architecture.** Annex III
high-risk attaches to systems influencing access to qualifications. `p538` filed
two German **offline** graders on exactly that reasoning. The same logic now has
a records half: [`DavidLMS/learnmcp-xapi`](https://github.com/DavidLMS/learnmcp-xapi)
(MIT) writes learner activity to an **xAPI-compliant LRS**, and the LRS its
README targets — **Ralph**, by **France Université Numérique** — is itself EMEA
and self-hostable. An auditable record held in the institution's own store is the
cheapest answer to a conformity assessment there is.

🟡 **The Gulf holding is unchanged and still rests on one weak source.** A
regional guide says Gulf states have moved fast on formal AI governance relative
to infrastructure, naming UAE bodies **KHDA** and the **UAE Ministry of
Education**. It is a content-marketing page. `p537`'s §9 finding stands: the Gulf
has **no open-source education platform layer at all**.
🟡 **UK regulation** reads as light-touch and principle-based through existing
regulators, with a consultation still open — from an unsourced comparison table,
so treat it as a lead.

> estate** (Moodle, ILIAS, Open edX) is what decides the boundary.

**Regulatory posture — re-verified from source this pass, with three dates
`p526` did not carry.** The **Digital Omnibus, Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**, was
published in the Official Journal on **24 July 2026** and has been in force since
**27 July 2026**. It moved the high-risk compliance deadline for stand-alone
Annex III education AI — grading, admissions triage, programme assignment, exam
proctoring, access to qualifications — from **2 August 2026 to 2 December 2027**.

| What | Date | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **Article 50 transparency** (tell users they are dealing with an AI) | **2 Aug 2026** | 🔴 **live now — not delayed** |
| Emotion-recognition AI in schools | Feb 2025 | 🔴 **banned — live now** |
| 🆕 Two new Article 5 prohibitions added by the Omnibus | **2 Dec 2026** | phasing in — ~5 weeks out |
| 🆕 National AI regulatory sandboxes | **2 Aug 2027** | deadline moved |
| **Annex III high-risk** (stand-alone education AI) | **2 Dec 2027** | moved from 2 Aug 2026 |
| 🆕 **Annex I** (AI as a safety component of a regulated product) | **2 Aug 2028** | |
| 🆕 **High-risk systems used by public authorities, including those already in service** | **2 Aug 2030** | separate, much later date |

🆕 **That last row is the most commercially significant line in this file for
EMEA public-sector work.** State schools and public universities *are* public
authorities across most of the EU. A system already in service there has until
**2 August 2030** — not December 2027. The practical consequence: **the private
and the public education buyer in the same country are on different clocks, more
than two and a half years apart.** Do not sell them the same urgency, and do not
let a public client be told they have 13 months when they may have 45.

**What that means commercially:** a **~25-month build window** on new high-risk
education AI, with transparency and the emotion-recognition ban live *now* and
two fresh prohibitions landing in about five weeks.

🆕 The **OECD 2026 Digital Education Outlook** reportedly recommends institutions
move **beyond general-purpose AI tools toward purpose-built educational AI** —
which is procurement language for "a generic chatbot will not clear review", and
the single best third-party sentence to quote into an EMEA pitch.

**Players.** Europe captured **62.3% of year-to-date 2026 edtech capital** (up
from 34.5% for full-year 2025) per trade aggregation. Named: **Multiverse**
(London, ~$70M at a ~$2.1B valuation), **Gizmo** (London, $22M Series A,
15 Apr 2026), **Ed.ai** (France, ~€5M seed, Sept 2026), **Lucida AI** ($5.4M seed,
Apr 2026, voice-first language coach), **Third Space Learning** ($5.94M growth
equity for *Skye*, a spoken maths tutor), plus AMBOSS, Preply and Knowunity.
Middle East and Africa are an order of magnitude smaller: **Dawraty** (Kuwait,
$2M seed, Qatar Development Bank), **3C Coding School** (Egypt, $3M seed for Saudi
expansion), **Eyone** (Dakar, Senegal). Gulf governance bodies moving unusually
fast relative to infrastructure: **KHDA** and the **UAE Ministry of Education**.

**Open-source anchors, placed:** [OpenOLAT](https://github.com/OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT)
(Apache-2.0, Switzerland), [ILIAS](https://github.com/ILIAS-eLearning/ILIAS)
(GPL-3.0, Germany), [Ralph LRS](https://github.com/openfun/ralph) (MIT, France),
[Dolos](https://github.com/dodona-edu/dolos) (MIT, Ghent — read from its
copyright line), [Open TutorAI CE](https://github.com/Open-TutorAi/open-tutor-ai-CE)
(BSD-3-Clause, **Morocco**), 🆕
[pedagogy-benchmark](https://github.com/AI-for-Education/pedagogy-benchmark)
(MIT, **Fab Inc**, UK-founded, LMIC focus) and 🆕 — read, not shipped —
[Lumen](https://github.com/ahmedEid1/lumen) (**GPL-3.0**, Essen, Germany).

**Opportunities.**
1. **AI Act conformity scaffolding, now priced per buyer type.** Dec 2027 for
   private providers, **Aug 2030 for public authorities' in-service systems**,
   Article 50 and the emotion-recognition ban enforceable today, two new
   prohibitions on 2 Dec 2026. An **audit-and-remediate engagement needs no new
   technology at all** and can start this quarter; the conformity build (technical
   documentation, logging, human oversight, risk management) follows on the clock
   that matches the client.
2. **Sovereign, self-hosted tutoring.** EU public-sector procurement rewards code
   provenance. OpenOLAT (Apache-2.0, Swiss) + a locally served model + Ralph for
   xAPI records is a stack with no US cloud dependency in the learning path — and
   the OECD "purpose-built, not general-purpose" line is the argument for it.
3. **MEA leapfrog, built on the Morocco row.** Open TutorAI CE is BSD-3-Clause
   with 194 forks against 114 stars — evidence of deployment, not hype. The
   natural base for francophone and Arabophone North Africa and Gulf work.
4. 🆕 **Pedagogical evaluation as a billable deliverable.** Fab Inc's CDPK/SEND
   benchmark is MIT, and **SEND** — special educational needs and disabilities —
   is where European regulators and parents apply the most pressure and where
   almost no vendor can show evidence. Running it is cheap; being the firm that
   ran it is the differentiator.

#### 🆕 `p529` — EMEA: money and guidance both moved this window

- 🟢 **UK — AI Adoption Summit commits £200m+**: **£100m** expanding *Bridge AI*
  and **£53m** for regional initiatives, delivered with **Cisco, IBM, BT and
  Rolls-Royce** as partners and **Skills England** setting the curriculum
  framework. ⚠️ Reported via a sector newsletter (QS/HolonIQ), not a Treasury
  document.
- 🟢 **EU — the Commission updated its ethical guidelines on AI in education
  (9 June)** and signed **three Advanced Digital Skills grant agreements worth
  €25.4m**. The **AI Continent Action Plan** is adopted and the **Apply AI
  Strategy** is under way.
- **Council of Europe** continues a dedicated **AI-and-education expert group**
  and working-conference track on the *regulatory* dimension — the body to watch
  for anything that becomes binding after Reg (EU) 2026/1744.
- **Adoption:** Eurobarometer — **64%** of Europeans agree everyone will need to
  be AI-literate by 2030. Allianz Pulse 2026 — **48%** use AI at least weekly
  across eight countries, with **Germany, France and Austria in the low 40s**.
  Eurostat via the EESC — **>50% of large enterprises** use AI against **<15% of
  SMEs**.
- **Market:** USD **512.6M (2024) → USD 1,328.8M (2029)**; **UK, Germany and
  France** named the leading markets.
- 🔵 **Read the contrast the sources themselves draw:** the UK is buying
  *deployment speed*, the EU is buying *institutional and human capacity*. An
  EMEA engagement is therefore sold differently on each side of the Channel —
  outcomes-per-quarter in the UK, auditability and capability-building in the EU.
  🟢 And this pass put the base's **first stated-origin EMEA institutional agent**
  on the shelf: [`thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem`](https://github.com/thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem),
  **Apache-2.0**, TH Mittelhessen — evidence that the EU capacity money lands as
  *university-built, permissively licensed* software.


#### 🆕 `p530` — no new regulatory material; the base is more precise than the sources

🔴 **The EMEA policy leg returned nothing this base did not already hold.** Every
substantive item the sweep surfaced is on this file or
[`open-gaps.md`](open-gaps.md) already: the **`Digital Omnibus`**
(Regulation (EU) **2026/1744**, OJ 24 July 2026) deferring high-risk education
duties — admissions, grading, placement, proctoring — to **2 December 2027**;
embedded-AI products to August 2028; the emotion-recognition ban live since
February 2025; Article 4 AI-literacy duties in force.

🟢 **And on the one date that matters commercially this base is *more* precise
than the sources.** Vendor and consultancy blogs paraphrase Article 50 as
applying "from 2 August 2026". This base holds the **marking grace as
`2026-12-02`**, two-sourced from `eur-lex`. 🔵 **Recorded as a positive
result:** on EU AI Act timing, the KB should be trusted over a secondary search,
and a pass that finds nothing new here has confirmed coverage rather than wasted
its budget.

🟢 **Where EMEA did move is supply, not policy** — and all of it permissive and
institutional: [`Selleo/mentingo`](https://github.com/Selleo/mentingo) (MIT,
Poland, the only permissive AI-native LMS), [`ls1intum/Artemis`](https://github.com/ls1intum/Artemis)
(MIT, TU München), [`numbas/Numbas`](https://github.com/numbas/Numbas)
(Apache-2.0, Newcastle University), [`openfun/richie`](https://github.com/openfun/richie)
(MIT, France Université Numérique), [`thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem`](https://github.com/thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem)
(Apache-2.0, TH Mittelhessen). 🔵 **EMEA is now this base's best-supplied region
for permissive, named-institution components** — which inverts the usual
framing, where EMEA appears as the regulated region and little else.



#### 🆕 `p537` — the Gulf, placed: highest adoption on this base, and almost nothing permissive to build on

🔴 **The Gulf is the sharpest adoption-to-supply inversion on these pages.**
`p535` measured the **UAE at ~75 % of teachers using AI in their work**, the highest
figure in this base's whole TALIS set. `Gap 536-D` asked what the region *ships*.
Answered on [`../repos/foundations.md` § Gulf-origin supply](../repos/foundations.md#-gulf-origin-supply--gap-536-d-answered-and-the-hole-is-a-grant-hole-p537):
nine repositories from the two leading Gulf AI institutions, of which **one** carries a
permissive grant.

- 🔴 **KAUST Academy (Saudi Arabia) ships curriculum under GPL-3.0** — 443★ / 88
  forks on the flagship course repository, four repositories at GPL-3.0 and three serving
  **no grant at any of 21 probed paths**. A client-branded fork of GPL-3.0 courseware is a
  GPL-3.0 distribution; so the engagement is **integrate-and-attribute, or negotiate a
  separate licence with the institution** — never fork.
- 🟢 **MBZUAI (UAE) ships one buildable asset of size**:
  [`mbzuai-oryx/groundingLMM`](https://github.com/mbzuai-oryx/groundingLMM) (Apache-2.0,
  969★), a grounded multimodal model — a *capability* layer, not an education product.
  Its newest agent framework, `OpenEarthAgent` (131★, ECCV 2026), serves no grant.
- 🔴 **No Arabic-language intelligent-tutoring repository was found**, by either the
  institutional or the full-text leg. Stated as a gap rather than left as silence.

🔵 **So the Gulf opportunity is not "fork a platform", which is what North America
and LATAM look like on this page.** It is (a) **licence and curriculum advisory** — four
of nine institutional repositories, two pushed within ten weeks, have no grant at all,
and an institution that wants adoption and has not noticed is a conversation; (b) **a
build on `groundingLMM`**, the one permissive asset; (c) **adapter work against national
systems**, as in EMEA proper. 🔴 **What it is not is a supply market.** Writing
"Gulf: high adoption, high opportunity" without this would be the error `536-D` existed
to prevent.

#### 🆕 `p542` — the regulatory calendar is filled in, and all of it was trending-only

- 🟡 **European Commission ethical guidelines on AI and data in teaching and
  learning — updated 2026**, now covering the **EU AI Act and generative AI**,
  with guiding questions, classroom scenarios, GDPR context and a revised
  glossary; aimed mainly at **primary and secondary** educators. Reachable
  sources disagree on the publication month (**March to June 2026**), and the
  disagreement is shown rather than resolved. **`unverified-at-primary`** —
  `education.ec.europa.eu` does not resolve from this environment.
- 🟡 **EC + OECD, *Empowering Learners for the Age of AI: An AI Literacy
  Framework for Primary and Secondary Education*, presented 18 June 2026.**
  **`unverified-at-primary`**. 🔵 **The most engagement-relevant item in this
  region**, because an AI-literacy *framework* is a curriculum and assessment
  deliverable, not a compliance one — it is scoped work with a named owner.
- 🟡 **Council of the EU `ST-9003-2026` (11 May 2026)** invites Member States
  and the Commission to support safe, inclusive AI use by teachers and asks the
  Commission to coordinate with the **AI Act's implementation in education**.
  **`unverified-at-primary`.**
- 🟡 **EU AI Act Article 50 transparency duties reported as applying since
  2 August 2026**, with May 2026 draft Commission guidance on high-risk
  classification said to include education examples. **`unverified-at-primary`**
  — attributed to a secondary compliance guide; **this base already holds the
  Article 50 span, and this is a date restatement, not a new claim.**
- 🟡 **National layer is thin and that is the finding.** Only **Ireland's Oide**
  guidance surfaced as a named national complement to the EU guidelines.
  🔴 **No other EMEA ministry's AI-in-education guidance was reachable on this
  pass** — an informed gap, not coverage: the region's *EU-level* calendar is
  now well mapped here and its **member-state** layer is not.
- 🔴 **And EMEA produced no EMEA-origin open-source artefact this pass.** The
  French and German legs returned supply, but of Chinese and unstated origin.
  **The region's build-ready assets on this base remain the EUPL public-sector
  registries filed at `p541` plus the on-premise Intel suite** — which is also
  the data-residency answer, and is Apache-2.0.

### APAC

🆕 **`p545` — demand leg: saturated.** The leg returned Korea's **AI Basic Act**, in force
**22 Jan 2026** and the closest thing in the region to the EU Act; **China** regulating by
service type (recommendation algorithms, deep synthesis, generative services, labelling of
AI-generated content) rather than by one statute; **Japan**'s promotion-first Act on the
Promotion of Research, Development and Utilisation of AI-Related Technologies
(**28 May 2025**), with no penalties; and **India** with no dedicated AI statute but a
binding labelling and provenance duty from **20 Feb 2026**. 🔵 **All already held.**
🔴 **The leg's own blank, stated rather than left silent: it returned no current
market-player analysis for AI education in any APAC country.** The regulatory picture is
well covered on this page and **the commercial picture is not**, and a tracker search is
not the instrument that will fix it.

#### 🆕 `p544` — APAC: regulation well documented, **demand still unmeasured for a third pass**

🔴 **The asymmetry is the finding and it has now held three passes:** this base
documents APAC *regulation* in detail — Korea's **AI Basic Act** (in force
`2026-01-22`, penalty grace through 2026), **Vietnam**'s education-as-high-risk
designation, **TEQSA**'s requirement that *every* Australian higher-education
provider file an institutional genAI action plan, China's filing and labelling
regime, Singapore's **AI Verify** — and holds **no K-12 or higher-education APAC
adoption rate at all**. 🔵 **Regulation without demand data is half a market map**,
and the half that is missing is the half a pitch needs.

🟢 **The strongest near-term offer is TEQSA-shaped and provider-wide by
construction:** the action-plan obligation applies to all providers, is
already executed, and is **documentation work with a named regulator and a
defined artefact** — the least speculative engagement shape in any region on this
page.

🔴 **Vietnam remains the clearest education-specific rule in APAC and the place
the ungranted-supply class was first measured** — three domestic education agents,
zero licences ([`measured-unusable.md`](measured-unusable.md)). 🔵 **Local
engineering capability is real there and is not licensed for reuse**, which is a
build-not-adopt signal rather than an absence of talent.


#### 🆕 `p543` — Vietnam has domestic education supply and no licences, which is itself the offer

🟢 **Three absent, substantive, education-specific Vietnamese agents**, found
on the Vietnamese-language leg and read first-hand:

| Asset | What it solves | Grant |
|---|---|---|
| [`MaiQuangPhuoc/edu_agents`](https://github.com/MaiQuangPhuoc/edu_agents) | Grade-10 **GDPT 2018** curriculum: Q&A, test generation, personalised paths. **Deliberately deterministic rather than ReAct, with the LLM bounded to the textbook.** | 🔴 **None** |
| [`dungptt-dng/smart-ict-classroom`](https://github.com/dungptt-dng/smart-ict-classroom) | Primary ICT lab with **fewer machines than groups**: fair turn scheduling, rotating in-group roles. Vietnam AI in Education Pioneer Award 2026 entry. | 🔴 **None** |
| [`minhdevtry/AI-Teacher-Assistant-Agent`](https://github.com/minhdevtry/AI-Teacher-Assistant-Agent) | Class management over **Zalo**, the national messaging platform: fee capture by text and OCR, arrears, invoices. | 🔴 **None** |

🔵 **Read together they describe a market, not three projects:** the binding
constraints are *scarce hardware*, *a prescribed national curriculum*, and *a
messaging platform rather than an LMS as the teacher's interface*. **None of
the 72 agents on this base's shelf is built for any of the three.**
🟢 **The engagement shape follows: licensing, governance and productisation of
domestic prototypes** — and the regulatory driver is already filed here,
**Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg**, which names education among six high-risk
sectors and makes automated assessment a compliance question.

🔴 **Said plainly: by artefact count Vietnam out-produced Türkiye this pass;
by *usable* artefact count it produced nothing.** A supply answer without the
grant is not an answer.

**🆕 `p541` — the region's supply keeps arriving under licences that are not
licences, and this pass can now name the rate.** Of the 37 unresolved grants
in p540's residue, the **source-available** class is disproportionately APAC:
[`heronerar/OpenGaokao`](https://github.com/heronerar/OpenGaokao)
(*Source-Available License 1.0*),
[`nakafaai/aksara`](https://github.com/nakafaai/aksara) (*Nakafa Source
Available 2.0*), [`QingdaoU/Judger`](https://github.com/QingdaoU/Judger)
(MIT body under the `SATA` title, which adds a non-OSI obligation), and
[`yuanjiusheng/cloud-learning-ce`](https://github.com/yuanjiusheng/cloud-learning-ce)
(**all rights reserved**, in Chinese). 🔴 **None is buildable, and all four
read as open at a glance** — three of them would pass a body-matching
classifier. 🟢 **The regional read:** APAC education supply is abundant and
its *grant* is the binding constraint, so an APAC engagement should budget a
licence-triage step before an architecture step. India contributed the one
promotable agent, [`Priyamakeshwari/TeachGPT`](https://github.com/Priyamakeshwari/TeachGPT)
(**MIT**, untitled body). Policy for the region is unchanged from `p540`.

**🆕 `p540` — one jurisdiction at a time, and Vietnam has the nearest hard
date.** Vietnam's AI law (in force 1 Mar 2026) names **education** in its
high-risk list and grants an **18-month** transition for systems already
operating in education, health care and finance — roughly **1 September
2027** — against 12 months elsewhere. That is a dated, sector-named obligation
to sell conformity work against. 🔴 **APAC is not converging:** South Korea has
a comprehensive statute with decrees still being finalised, Australia's
National AI Plan (Dec 2025) deliberately relies on existing law, and Japan
remains on voluntary guidelines. Build the compliance layer as **pluggable
per-jurisdiction policy**, never as one regional module. 🔴 **India returned
nothing on this pass's leg** despite being named a dominant regional market —
an open blank, not a judgement about India.

🆕 **`p539` — APAC is the only region where education is *named* in a
high-risk list, and it is also where the biggest permissive asset turned up.**

| Jurisdiction | Instrument | Education-specific? |
|---|---|---|
| **South Korea** | **AI Basic Act**, in force **22 Jan 2026**. MSIT has signalled 2026 is effectively a **pilot year with limited enforcement**; enforcement decrees still being finalised | no |
| **Vietnam** | AI law effective **1 Mar 2026**, risk-based. 🔴 **Education is in the high-risk list** — automated assessment and behavioural uses, one of six named sectors | 🟢 **yes** |
| **China** | the most extensive framework: **mandatory registration** and strict **content-labelling** obligations | no, but binding on any deployed tutor |
| **Japan** | cooperation and existing law rather than penalties | no |
| **Singapore** | frameworks and toolkits — **AI Verify**; consulting on AI risk-management guidelines, response possibly late 2026 | no |
| **Australia** | 🟢 **TEQSA required all higher-education providers to submit institutional action plans** on generative-AI risk | 🟢 **yes, and already executed** |

🟢 **Vietnam and Australia are the two most actionable regulatory facts in this
whole file**, because both name education and both have passed their date. An
Australian university has already filed a plan; the delivery work against that
plan is unbuilt.

🟢 **Supply: [`netless-io/flat`](https://github.com/netless-io/flat) — MIT,
6 400★ · 876 forks, the largest permissive education-specific codebase on this
base outside the tutoring shelf**, and it sat unfiled in this base's own history.
Agora/Shengwang's open-source classroom: whiteboard, Electron desktop clients,
**cloud recording**. 🔵 The recording artefact is the seam — a recorded class plus
its whiteboard stream is the exact input the shelf's video-to-courseware agents
want. 🔴 **The real-time transport is Agora's commercial SDK**: the MIT grant
covers the client, not the media plane.

🟡 **Market players for the region come from a single commercial research page**
— Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson, Byju's — with adoption described as strongest
in **China, India and Japan**. One source, commercial, rankings unverified;
recorded because the absence of any player list would read as absence of players.
🔵 **And the EU AI Act reaches APAC vendors** selling into Europe, so the 2 Dec
2027 Annex III date is an APAC export constraint as well as an EMEA one.

> 🆕 **`p538` — APAC supplied the queue's highest-starred row, and it is not an
> agent.**

> 国家中小学智慧教育平台 (`basic.smartedu.cn`), the PRC's national K-12 platform,
> decrypting video and merging to MP4. 🟢 **Read it against China's compulsory
> AI-from-age-six policy recorded above:** the policy creates demand for
> curriculum-grounded tutoring, and this is the only tool on this base that
> acquires a **national curriculum corpus** at scale. ⚠️ The MIT grant covers the
> **tool**; the platform's terms on the **content** are unread here, and that
> distinction is the whole legal question for a commercial deployment. 🟢 Also
> placed: [`wenflow-org/wenflow`](https://github.com/wenflow-org/wenflow) (MIT,
> 61★ — goal-first learning paths with spaced review and *simulated virtual
> learners* for testing the loop without real students),
> [`zhangl1001/civil-ai`](https://github.com/zhangl1001/civil-ai) (MIT, 41★ —

> civil-service exam as its worked vertical),
> [`tanghua-git/skill-instructional-design`](https://github.com/tanghua-git/skill-instructional-design)
> (lesson plans against named pedagogy — ICAP, Bloom, 扶放有度 — 🟡 MIT in the
