> (Taiwan; MIT; de-identification as **one static HTML file, no backend**) and
> [`guruduan/SIMG`](https://github.com/guruduan/SIMG) (Indonesia; MIT; a Moodle
> plugin encoding BK counselling and *surat izin siswa*, notifying over
> **WhatsApp**). 🔵 **The regional lesson in that last one is the channel:** an
> engagement that assumes email in Indonesia has lost the teachers before it
> starts. 🔴 **One ungranted artefact worth knowing about rather than using:**
> [`Traceable-Ideological-Education-RAG`](https://github.com/upstream1119/Traceable-Ideological-Education-RAG)
> has **no licence at all**, and is the only thing here treating citation
> grounding and a policy check as first-class architecture — which is precisely
> the primitive a regulated-curriculum engagement in this region needs.


> 🆕 **`p536` — APAC is where the filing-rate defect cost the most, and the
> correction is worth stating as money.** 🔴 [`pupilfirst/pupilfirst`](https://github.com/pupilfirst/pupilfirst)
> is **MIT with 980★ and 279 forks** — an LMS built around **coaching, submission
> review and feedback queues** rather than content delivery, from Pupilfirst Pvt. Ltd.
> (India) — and this base held it only inside a sentence, because its licence is **404
> on `main` / 200 on `master`** and the API grades it `NOASSERTION`. 🟡 It is **~11
> months stale**, so the play is fork-and-own; the coaching data model is the asset.
> 🟢 **The government layer is the genuinely new opening.**
> [`nso-india/esankhyiki-mcp`](https://github.com/nso-india/esankhyiki-mcp) is **MIT,
> 147★, and its copyright line is the Ministry of Statistics and Programme
> Implementation** — an *official* national MCP server. 🔵 **Use it twice:** as the
> grounding layer for any India engagement that must cite the government's own
> enrolment, attainment or expenditure figures, and as the **precedent to show other
> ministries** when proposing they publish one. 🟢 Alongside it:
> [`microsoft/Shiksha-Copilot`](https://github.com/microsoft/Shiksha-Copilot) (MIT, 9★,
> pushed 2026-10-28) targets the **state-board government-school teacher**, which is a
> buyer segment nothing else on this base addresses; `hantang/smartedu-dl-go` (MIT,
> 207★) is the ingest tool for China's national K-12 platform; `RozhakDev/EduPintarIPAS`
> (MIT) joins `naufaldi/teacher-exam` to give **Indonesia a teacher-facing and a
> pupil-facing artefact**, both naming the national curriculum by its own acronym.
> 🟢 **And a new capability band with APAC origin:**
> [`GeminiLight/SocialCoach`](https://github.com/GeminiLight/SocialCoach) (Apache-2.0,
> **459★**) is social-emotional learning — conflict role-play with feedback — which no
> other row on this base does, and which is a funded line in both US K-12 and EU
> wellbeing frameworks. 🔵 Against a demand side reported at **24.8 % CAGR
> (2026–2033), the fastest of any region** — single-channel, and labelled as such.


> 🟢 **`p535` — the "no APAC adoption rate exists" blank is filled, and the number is a
> spread, not a point.** TALIS 2024: **Singapore ~75%** of teachers using AI in their
> work, **Japan <20%** — the joint highest and joint lowest of any system measured, in
> one region. 🔵 Any APAC proposal that quotes a regional average is quoting something
> no buyer in the region recognises. 🔴 Search-backend only; `oecd.org` is `ENOTFOUND`.

> 🆕 **`p534` — commercial and policy motion, no education-specific regulation,
> and no new addresses.** **TCS × Pearson** signed a multi-year AI learning
> alliance aimed at employer skills gaps; **LearnUpon** opened a Sydney HQ with
> AI course-authoring; **OpenAI appointed a policy lead for Australia and New
> Zealand** as Canberra tightens AI governance and copyright. 🔴 The only named
> regulator activity this leg returned — Singapore's consultations — is
> **financial-sector, not education**, and last pass's Korean AI Basic Act and
> Vietnamese high-risk designation did not reappear; 🔵 treat that as a channel
> artefact, not a repeal. 🟡 One forecast puts APAC as the **fastest-growing
> region at 24.8 % CAGR (2026–2033)**, which is consistent with this base's
> observation that APAC already holds its largest *supply* of education agents
> (DeepTutor, OpenMAIC, `dsh-openmaic`, Study-Mate, `ai-shifu`, `teacher-exam`).

> 🆕 **`p533` — supply added on this leg.** 🟢 [`naufaldi/teacher-exam`](https://github.com/naufaldi/teacher-exam)
> (*Ujian Sekolah*, **MIT**) generates **A4 print-ready exam sheets for
> Indonesian elementary-school teachers** — mixed item types, answer key and a
> *pembahasan* for every question — from a one-sentence brief, live at
> `ujian-sekolah.naufaldi.com`. 🔵 **Read the problem statement it names, because
> it is a market fact and not a feature list:** Indonesian SD teachers *"spend
> 4–8 hours a week hand-writing exams in Word."* 🟢 **The output is paper**, which
> is the right answer in a context where the constraint is a photocopier rather
> than a device per student — the same insight `P600` draws for rural LATAM,
> arrived at independently in APAC. 🔴 Note the asymmetry this leg keeps
> producing: APAC supplies the largest agents on this base (DeepTutor 49.6k★,
> OpenMAIC 41.8k★) **and its classroom demand remains unmeasured here.**

> 🆕 **`p531` — supply placed, and it is the only region shipping full agents.**
> [`THU-MAIC/dsh-openmaic`](https://github.com/THU-MAIC/dsh-openmaic) (MIT,
> **111★**, Tsinghua MAIC — classrooms, slides, Socratic teaching as a harness
> plugin) joins this base's two largest education agents, both APAC-academic.
> 🟢 **Two state-infrastructure signals that have no analogue elsewhere:**
> [`nso-india/esankhyiki-mcp`](https://github.com/nso-india/esankhyiki-mcp)
> (**MIT, 147★**) is an MCP server published by **India's Ministry of Statistics
> and Programme Implementation** — a national statistical office shipping an
> agent interface under a permissive licence; and a cluster of tooling around
> **China's national smart-education platform**
> ([`alterem/knowledge-grab`](https://github.com/alterem/knowledge-grab), MIT,
> **567★**; [`hantang/smartedu-dl-go`](https://github.com/hantang/smartedu-dl-go),
> MIT, **207★**) whose existence is itself the measurement: the state platform is
> where the content is. 🔵 Further placed supply:
> [`oas114/EduShield`](https://github.com/oas114/EduShield) (MIT, Taiwan —
> client-side de-identification before student data reaches a model),
> [`RozhakDev/EduPintarIPAS`](https://github.com/RozhakDev/EduPintarIPAS)
> (Indonesia), [`guruduan/SIMG`](https://github.com/guruduan/SIMG) (Indonesia,
> a Moodle administration plugin with WhatsApp notifications),
> [`localis-ai/student-discovery-agent`](https://github.com/localis-ai/student-discovery-agent)
> (Vietnam, career guidance) and
> [`Turkish-AI-Community/hsd-agu-egitim`](https://github.com/Turkish-AI-Community/hsd-agu-egitim)
> (Türkiye, 33★). 🔴 **The opportunity:** APAC is the only region where the
> *agent* can be reused rather than built — and the integration risk moves to
> the state platform and the local messaging channel (WhatsApp, Feishu), not the
> LMS.

**Regulatory posture — no single model.** Binding AI rules: **South Korea, China,
Vietnam**. Guidance and existing law: **Japan, Singapore, Hong Kong, Australia,
India**. **South Korea's AI Basic Act** is the region's landmark statute (sources
disagree on whether it is the region's first or the world's second comprehensive
AI law — the framing, not the fact, is in dispute). China regulates recommendation
algorithms, deep synthesis, generative AI services and **AI-generated-content
labelling**, with a non-binding AI-agent opinion effective **15 July 2026**. Japan
is promotion-led — its 2025 AI Promotion Act carries no explicit penalties.
**Australia's National AI Plan relies on existing law** rather than a standalone
act. **India requires prominent labels and provenance markers on AI-generated
content from 20 Feb 2026** and *plans* compulsory AI and computational thinking
**from Class 3 in the 2026-27 academic year**.

🆕 **Vietnam's risk-based AI law gives education a longer runway than other
sectors: an 18-month transition period for systems already in service.** That is
the kind of sector-specific carve-out that decides a sequencing decision, and it
makes Vietnam the easier APAC entry point for a product already running elsewhere.

⚠️ *Sources conflict on curriculum status: one claims China and the UAE are the
only countries running compulsory national AI curricula since the 2025-26 school
year, which sits badly with India's rollout being described as planned. UNESCO
separately reports China, Japan, Korea and Singapore integrating AI curricula, and
notes that **adoption needs infrastructure, connectivity and teacher training, not
just policy** — in much of the region basic educational needs come first. Verify
per country before quoting.*

**Players.** **TAL** (China, pivoted into AI learning hardware), **ByteDance
Gauth**, **PhysicsWallah** (India, own AI tutor). 🆕 **Public infrastructure is
the scale player India's commercial market cannot match: NCERT's DIKSHA** already
reaches teachers and students nationally. **SEAMEO INNOTECH** is the regional
body for Southeast Asia. Sector context matters: China shut its ~$100B private
tutoring industry in 2021 and India's BYJU'S collapsed into insolvency — the
market is rebuilding around AI tutoring from a low base of trust. Ipsos Education
Monitor 2026 finds **lower** support for banning AI in schools across Asian
markets, with **Australia and New Zealand more** supportive of a ban.

**Open-source anchors, placed — the strongest supply position of any region.**
Four of the thirteen agents on [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md) are APAC,
including both of the two largest in the world:
[DeepTutor](https://github.com/HKUDS/DeepTutor) (Apache-2.0, **49.0k★**, Univ. of
Hong Kong) and [OpenMAIC](https://github.com/THU-MAIC/OpenMAIC) (MIT, **41.8k★**,
Tsinghua), plus 🆕 [lineage-skill](https://github.com/JuneYaooo/lineage-skill)
(Apache-2.0, 462★, China) and
[Educhain](https://github.com/satvik314/educhain) (MIT, 387★, **India** — placed
this pass off its licence-file copyright line, `educhain.in`). **This is still
the single most actionable supply fact in this file.**

**Opportunities.**
1. **Build on the APAC agents, not around them.** DeepTutor and OpenMAIC are
   Apache-2.0 and MIT, both shipped releases on 28-29 Oct 2026, and together carry
   **90.8k stars**. Anywhere in the world, the fastest credible tutoring prototype
   starts from one of these two.
2. **Content-provenance compliance as a product.** India (20 Feb 2026) and China
   both require AI-content labelling. Generated lesson material needs provenance
   metadata baked in at authoring time. 🆕 **`lineage-skill` is the on-ramp:** it
   is Apache-2.0 and already produces **source-backed** lessons — provenance is
   its design, not a retrofit. Emit H5P with that provenance attached and the
   labelling obligation is satisfied structurally rather than by policy.
3. **India's Class-3 curriculum rollout, through DIKSHA.** A compulsory AI subject
   from Class 3 across 2026-27 is a teacher-training and
   content-at-national-scale problem, and DIKSHA is the distribution that already
   exists. Kolibri (MIT, offline-first) plus a content-generation agent addresses
   the connectivity constraint that will otherwise decide the outcome.
4. **ANZ needs the restraint story, not the capability story.** The one market in
   this region where public opinion leans toward *banning* AI in schools. Lead
   with controls, provenance and integrity tooling
   ([Dolos](https://github.com/dodona-edu/dolos), MIT).
5. 🆕 **Vietnam first for a port.** The 18-month education transition is the
   softest landing in the region for a system already in service.

#### 🆕 `p529` — APAC: fastest-growing, and still no common framework

- **Market:** USD **591.6M (2024) → USD 1,848.1M (2029)**, ~20.9% CAGR —
  **named the fastest-growing region** on both methodologies checked, driven by
  internet penetration and government-led digital-education reform.
- **Regulation stays fragmented.** Forrester: *"a common APAC-wide AI legislative
  framework will remain a distant dream"* — **Singapore** leaning on mature
  guidelines, **China** targeting algorithmic misconduct, **ASEAN**'s governance
  guide still early-stage. ⚠️ Nothing education-specific surfaced beyond what
  this base already holds (China's compulsory AI from age six, Beijing's 8h,
  India's Class 3 target, Singapore AI Verify).
- **2026 posture:** APAC leadership attention is on **governance, AI literacy,
  sovereignty and measurable ROI**. Enterprise deployment runs **ahead** of
  Europe and North America — **56%** of APAC businesses have deployed chatbots,
  copilots or assistants (Confluent). ⚠️ Enterprise figures, not school figures.
- 🔵 **The supply side corroborates the demand side here.** APAC ships **5** of
  the per-university Moodle bridges in the census head (Vietnam, Sri Lanka,
  China, Malaysia, Australia) — second only to LATAM — and the two largest
  education agents in existence are APAC-built (`DeepTutor`, HKU; `OpenMAIC`,
  Tsinghua: **49,216★** and **41,756★**). **APAC is not an adoption market for
  this base's shelf — it is where the shelf comes from.**


#### 🆕 `p530` — mandates are the demand driver, and the teacher-training gap is the opening

| Country | Instrument | Figure |
|---|---|---|
| **China** | AI education compulsory across national K-12 | **≥ 8 hours/year** mandated for primary and secondary (Beijing); national plan extends AI into **teacher qualification exams**, full AI-literacy infrastructure targeted **2030** |
| **India** | AI + Computational Thinking mandatory **from Class 3**, academic year **2026–27**, under NEP 2020 | 🔴 **~10 million teachers** need upskilling |
| **Singapore** | AI modules in primary computer science; AI training for teachers at every level by **2026**; supervised use via Student Learning Space | — |
| Regional attitude | Ipsos Education Monitor 2026 — support for **banning** AI in schools | 🟢 **23–31%** across Asian markets vs **39% global**; Singapore **37% → 28%** YoY; India **26%** |

🟢 **The opportunity is the inverse of the mandate.** Curricula are legislated and
the teacher capacity to deliver them is not. India's ~10 million-teacher gap and
China's move to test AI competence in **teacher qualification exams** both point
at the same product: **teacher-facing training and assessment**, at national
scale, under a permissive licence a ministry can self-host.

🟢 **Matching supply this base verified this pass:**
[`microsoft/Shiksha-Copilot`](https://github.com/microsoft/Shiksha-Copilot) (MIT,
teacher copilot for Indian classrooms),
[`Sunbird-Ed/SunbirdEd-portal`](https://github.com/Sunbird-Ed/SunbirdEd-portal)
(MIT, India's national stack, **317 forks against 41 stars**),
[`THU-MAIC/OpenMAIC`](https://github.com/THU-MAIC/OpenMAIC) (MIT, Tsinghua,
41.8k★), [`ai-shifu/ai-shifu`](https://github.com/ai-shifu/ai-shifu)
(Apache-2.0). 🔵 **Sunbird is the sovereignty play**: an MIT national platform a
ministry already forks is a far easier procurement than a vendor product.

🟡 **Caveat carried.** Every figure above is from secondary commentary, not
ministry documents. The China hours figure and the India Class-3 date should be
confirmed against primary sources before they go in a client deck.


#### 🆕 `p542` — the richest leg of the four, and two national programmes this base never held

- 🔴 **China — MOE's 2026 digital-education priorities, absent from this base
  until now.** A ministry meeting (reported **31 March 2026**) set 2026
  priorities around integrating AI across **teaching, learning and
  management**, supporting personalised learning, building interdisciplinary
  skills, and explicitly **strengthening data security and personal
  information protection**. A separate **AI + Education Action Plan**, dated
  **2 April 2026 and issued by five departments**, is also reported.
  **`unverified-at-primary`** — the action plan comes from a commercial
  education guide and `en.moe.gov.cn` was not reachable from this environment.
  🔵 **The data-security clause is the commercially important half:** it means
  a China-facing education build is governed on *data handling* as much as on
  pedagogy, which is the same constraint shape as EMEA's and not the usual
  framing of this market.
- 🔴 **Singapore — the AIEd Framework and EdTech Masterplan 2030, both
  absent.** The **AI in Education (AIEd) Framework** rests on four principles —
  **Agency, Inclusivity, Fairness and Safety** — and sits inside the broader
  **EdTech Masterplan 2030**. **`unverified-at-primary`** (commercial blog).
  🟡 The *"Four Learns"* framing this base holds in trending — *learn about AI,
  learn to use AI, learn with AI, and most importantly learn beyond AI*, set
  out by Minister of State Jasmin Lau at the **March 2026** Committee of Supply
  — is the public articulation of that framework.
- 🟢 **A concrete, actionable procurement rule, and it is first-order for a
  build:** MOE states schools should use AI **selectively**, only where it
  contributes meaningfully to learning outcomes, and that for commercial
  off-the-shelf tools schools **must check that input data contains no
  personally identifiable information** and complies with data-management
  guidelines. 🔵 **That is a PII-stripping requirement on the ingress path of
  any tool sold into Singapore schools** — an architectural constraint, not a
  policy sentiment, and it is satisfied by on-premise or edge inference far
  more cheaply than by a hosted API.
- 🟡 **Entry age varies and is contested.** An MOE parliamentary reply notes
  that some systems — **China, the UAE and the United Kingdom** — introduce AI
  from **Primary 1**; Singapore's **Primary 4** entry point has been questioned
  in Parliament. A commercial guide's claim that Singapore withholds AI tools
  from Primary 1–3 **could not be confirmed** and is recorded as unconfirmed.
- 🔴 **ASEAN Guide on AI Governance and Ethics — absent from this base**, and
  reported as still early-stage, with the region expected to converge on common
  principles (safety, transparency, accountability) while executing them
  per-market. 🔵 **The practical consequence for a multi-country APAC
  engagement is that there is no single conformity target** — which is what
  this base's APAC pattern already assumes, and now has a named instrument for.
- 🟢 **Supply remains APAC's strength, and this pass re-confirmed it
  first-hand:** `OpenMAIC` (Tsinghua, **MIT**, 41.8k★, 6.5k forks, 805
  commits) and `dsh-openmaic`, against `Agent4Edu` (USTC-implied, AAAI 2025
  Oral) which is **ungranted and therefore not buildable**. **The region
  produces the assets; the licence quality is uneven and must be read per
  repo.**

### Global

**🆕 `p541` — one global number this pass can stand behind, and one it refuses
to average.** 🟢 The **OECD 2026 Digital Education Outlook** recommends
institutions move from general-purpose AI toward **purpose-built educational
AI** — which is the demand-side statement of this base's whole thesis, and it
is already held here. 🔴 **The market-size figures surfaced this pass
disagree too widely to publish as one band**: one source projects roughly
**$6 B (2024) → $32 B (2030)**, against the **$9.58 B → $42.48 B (2030, 41.5 %
CAGR)** band this file already carries from better-attributed sources, and a
third vendor page asserted precise figures with no method at all. **The
disagreement is shown rather than averaged**, per this file's rule — an
averaged number is a number nobody published. 🔵 The usable global signal is
not a size but a **shape**: adoption is ahead of governance everywhere it was
measured this pass, in every region, by a margin of 25–60 percentage points.

**🆕 `p540` — the forks-per-star rule, offered as an estimating aid rather
than a market claim.** On fourteen points read first-hand this pass,
deploy-per-institution software sat at **f/★ ≥ 0.25** and import-as-dependency
software at **≤ 0.182**, with an empty band between
([`../evidence/forks-per-star-rank.2026-10-30-p540.tsv`](../evidence/forks-per-star-rank.2026-10-30-p540.tsv)).
🔵 **Use it to scope, not to choose:** above the line, budget for carrying a
fork; below it, budget for version drift. 🔴 **n = 14, one sector, one hour** —
`Gap 540-C` re-measures at n ≥ 60 and no recipe may depend on the threshold
until it does. 🔴 **And it ranks without vetting:** the second-placed repository
on that ranking describes itself as *"Deprecated"*.

> 🆕 **`p531` — the cross-region finding is a licence instrument, not a market
> number.** The licence-compliance layer every region's deliverable needs is
> permissive, mature and was sitting in this base's own discarded history:
> [`aboutcode-org/scancode-toolkit`](https://github.com/aboutcode-org/scancode-toolkit)
> (Apache-2.0 code / 🟡 CC-BY-4.0 datasets, **2 656★**),
> [`oss-review-toolkit/ort`](https://github.com/oss-review-toolkit/ort)
> (Apache-2.0, **2 109★**), [`anchore/syft`](https://github.com/anchore/syft)
> (Apache-2.0, **9 792★**) and
> [`CycloneDX/cyclonedx-cli`](https://github.com/CycloneDX/cyclonedx-cli)
> (Apache-2.0, 552★). 🔵 In a sector whose platform cores are AGPL-3.0 and whose
> integration layer is permissive, **"which grant does this deliverable actually
> carry, transitively" is a question every engagement answers once and bills
> for** — and it needs a retrieval, not a build.

🆕 **`p530`.** Opportunities that are not region-specific, kept here so the four
regional sections stay regional rather than absorbing cross-cutting work.

| Opportunity | Why it is global | Components verified this pass |
|---|---|---|
| **The standards integration layer** | SCORM, xAPI and LTI are international standards; an institution on any continent speaks them, and **eleven of the twelve implementations are MIT / Apache-2.0 / BSD** against a platform layer that is almost entirely copyleft | [`scorm-again`](https://github.com/jcputney/scorm-again), [`lrsql`](https://github.com/yetanalytics/lrsql), [`tsugi`](https://github.com/tsugiproject/tsugi), [`XBlock`](https://github.com/openedx/XBlock) — [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md) |
| **Permissive autograding** | Assessment mechanics are subject-bound, not territory-bound. 🔴 **And the market's own discovery channel reports this capability as absent** — a generic search for an open-source grader returned *"I found no open-source agent that grades students"* against **2 337★** of permissive autograders | [`nbgrader`](https://github.com/jupyter/nbgrader) (BSD-3, 1 370★), [`Submitty`](https://github.com/Submitty/Submitty) (BSD-3, 806★), [`otter-grader`](https://github.com/ucbds-infra/otter-grader) (BSD-3, 161★) |
| **The SIS agent** | No agent speaks to any SIS in any region, and as of `p530` there is a **permissive** SIS to build one against — so the whole stack can ship under MIT | [`academico`](https://github.com/academico-sis/academico) (MIT, 409★) + **P559** |
| **Mastery estimation with a literature** | *"Is this student at risk"* must survive a faculty board in every jurisdiction; BKT predates the LLM era and has citations | [`pyBKT`](https://github.com/CAHLR/pyBKT) (MIT), [`pykt-toolkit`](https://github.com/pykt-team/pykt-toolkit) (MIT) |

🟡 **Unplaced supply, recorded here rather than guessed into a region** — all
MIT, all verified first-hand this pass, none stating a location:
[`zijinz456/OpenTutor`](https://github.com/zijinz456/OpenTutor) (156★),
[`DozenTwelve/Papermorph`](https://github.com/DozenTwelve/Papermorph) (1 015★),
[`wenflow-org/wenflow`](https://github.com/wenflow-org/wenflow) (61★ · learning
paths with forgetting-curve review and load tracking, Vue3 + SQLite,
self-hostable — the author calls it an early experimental prototype) and
[`zhangl1001/civil-ai`](https://github.com/zhangl1001/civil-ai) (41★ ·
**local-first** adaptive tutoring agent for iOS and Web, with a civil-service
exam application). 🔵 **An author's name, a README's language and a domain are
not a country** — this base's standing rule, and the reason these are here and
not under APAC.

#### 🆕 `p542` — a commercial reading of the corpus finding

- 🟢 **The strongest transferable result this pass produced is not a market
  number, it is a scanning defect with a price.** Running this base's own
  discovery legs in nine languages showed that **what an English-keyed scan
  returns is the English-publishing corpus**, and that languages whose
  developers publish into GitHub in English (**German, Japanese, Korean —
  0 new artefacts of 3**) behave completely differently from languages with a
  separate corpus (**Indonesian and Chinese, 2 of 2 each**; Spanish and
  Portuguese, 8 of 10 at `p541`).
- 🔵 **Why that is a client-facing finding and not housekeeping:** any client
  doing build-versus-buy or competitive scanning in an education market
  **is running an English-keyed scan**, because that is what every tool
  defaults to. In Indonesia, China, Brazil or Mexico that scan **systematically
  under-reports local open-source supply and local competitors** — and the
  client cannot see the omission, because a missing result looks exactly like
  an absent one. 🟢 **"We scan your market in its own language" is a
  differentiator that this pass measured rather than asserted**, and the
  measurement is cheap to reproduce in front of a buyer.
- 🔴 **With its hard limit named:** this base already holds Chinese
  **GitHub-hosted** supply (`smartedu-dl-go`, `knowledge-grab`, `schoolcms`
  and others), so the gap is narrower and sharper than "China is unreached" —
  it is the part of the local supply that sits on **Chinese forges**
  (**GitLink, Gitee**), which are **unreachable from this environment**:
  `gitlink.org.cn` does not resolve, `gitee.com` is refused at the gateway.
  🔵 **So the claim has a boundary: scanning in-language closes the *query*
  gap and does not close the *access* gap.** A China-market scan needs network
  reach into Chinese forges, which is a procurement and policy question before
  it is a research one. **`Gap 542-D`.**
- 🟢 **Three permissive, globally-placed assets were added this pass** —
  `DaRL-GenAI/instructional_agents` (MIT, faculty workload),
  `minmaxflow/mini-kode` (MIT, reference agent loop),
  `ocademy-ai/machine-learning` (MIT code + CC-BY-4.0 content) — and
  `Intelligent-Internet/ii-agent` (Apache-2.0) as substrate. ⚠️ All four carry
  **no stated country**, which is why they are Global and not placed: **origin
  is read here, never inferred.**

### LATAM

🆕 **`p545` — demand leg: saturated on regulation, and the "void" `p543`/`p544` named is
narrower than it looked.** The leg returned **Peru** and **El Salvador** as the only
states with a specific AI law fully in force; Brazil's **PL 2338/2023**, approved by the
Senate in December 2024 and **still pending in the Câmara**; Brazil's **MEC** guidance as
*orientative, not binding*; **Mexico** with no national law or binding university
guidance; and **Colombia** among the regional leaders on framework-building. 🔵 **All
already held.**

🟢 **But the void itself needs correcting, and the correction came from the supply leg,
not this one.** `p544` recorded *"no education-specific national rule for Brazil or
Mexico surfaced"*. 🔴 **For Brazil that is wrong, and this base already held the
counter-example without noticing it:** **Lei nº 15.100 of 13 January 2025** (DOU
14/01/2025) is a Brazilian **federal statute about education specifically** — it bans
personal devices in *educação básica* during class, break and intervals, **excepting
strictly pedagogical, teacher-mediated use**, and obliges school networks to publish
mental-health strategies. 🟢 It is on this page already, two thousand lines below,
attributed to [`maiatechdev/avit`](https://github.com/maiatechdev/avit) — **a repository
whose entire product is the exception clause.**

🔵 **The correct statement of the LATAM regulation gap is therefore narrower and more
useful: what is missing is not education-specific national rules — Brazil has one in force
— but *AI*-specific ones.** Lei 15.100/2025 is a device statute that an AI product must
live inside; it says nothing about models, assessment or automated decisions. 🟡 **For
Mexico the original claim stands**: no national law, no binding university guidance.

🔴 **Two LATAM figures are recorded as NOT adopted.** A secondary source attributes to
UNESCO that *"LATAM has the world's highest generative-AI adoption among university
students while only 19 % of institutions have an approved internal rule"* — the shape is
plausible and the original report was not reached. And a single outlet reports a
**Colombian MinEducación June-2026 roadmap to 2028 with USD 412 M** attached; **the
investment figure could not be corroborated against any official source** and is not
adopted.

#### 🆕 `p544` — LATAM: the governance deficit is the offer, and the regulation void is **still** a void

🟢 **The most quotable pair of numbers on this page remains UNESCO IESALC +
UNU-IAS, and it reproduced again this pass:** **87 %** of 200 institutions across
19 countries use AI somewhere, **26 %** have a formal AI strategy. 🔵 **A
61-point governance deficit, from a named primary with a stated sampling frame** —
which is why it can be put in front of a client where this page's market-size
bands cannot. 🟢 The study's own recommendation set (national AI strategies that
include higher education, dedicated funding, national guidance, AI literacy and
governance written into accreditation) is effectively **a scope of work already
endorsed by the region's reference institution**.

🔴 **And the void is unchanged:** asked directly for Brazilian and Mexican
education-AI rules, the leg returned **PL 2338 still pending with no
education-specific provision** and **nothing whatsoever for Mexico**. 🔵 **So
LATAM institutional demand is evidenced and LATAM national education-AI regulation
is not** — an engagement here is sold on institutional governance, not on
compliance with a rule that does not yet exist. 🔴 Adoption *"driven by individual
academics, researchers and students rather than institution-wide strategies"* is
the mechanism behind the 87/26 split, and it means the buyer is usually the
**institution trying to catch up with its own faculty**.


#### 🆕 `p543` — the leg returned nothing absent, and the one promotion is a demand figure

🔴 **An informed blank, not silence.** The LATAM leg returned the UNESCO
IESALC study, the Universidad de los Andes **716-instrument** count, Uruguay's
**Ceibal**, Brazil's **Cetic.br**, Chile's **CENIA** and the **Tec de
Monterrey**–UNESCO agreement. 🟢 **Every one was already filed on this page** —
graded `FILED`, not merely recognised, by
[`../ingest/claim-role.sh`](../ingest/claim-role.sh).

🟡 **One claim promoted out of trending history:** the **Digital Education
Council's 2026 LATAM survey** — **92 % of students and 79 % of faculty**
actively engaging with AI. 🔵 **Beside the IESALC institutional figure (87 % of
~200 institutions in 19 countries using AI somewhere, **26 %** with a formal
framework) it sharpens the pitch this base has made for several passes:** the
region's gap is **not adoption and not enthusiasm — it is the governance
artefact**, and the governance artefact is a deliverable.

🔵 **What LATAM did *not* produce this pass, and it is worth naming:** no new
domestic open-source supply. `p541`'s Spanish and Portuguese legs produced
eight absent artefacts; the English-language regional leg produces none. **The
language of the query, not the region of the question, is what moves the supply
side here.**

**🆕 `p541` — the LATAM supply gap this base has declared for many passes was
substantially an instrument artefact, and here is the measurement.** Running
the mandated supply legs in **Portuguese and Spanish** returned **eight
addresses absent from this base out of ten**, against **zero of five** in
English. 🟢 **What they are**, with grants read first-hand as bytes:
[`UFPA-LAMIC/EduAcess`](https://github.com/UFPA-LAMIC/EduAcess) — **MIT**,
from Universidade Federal do Pará, converting course PDFs into **narrated
audio and optimised text** for students with visual impairment, ADHD, autism
and dyslexia, the only **accessibility-first** artefact on this base;
[`Ignacio-Ghub/asistente-docente-sin-conexion`](https://github.com/Ignacio-Ghub/asistente-docente-sin-conexion)
— **GPL-3.0**, a teacher assistant that runs a local model with **no internet
and no data leaving the device**, aimed at rural schools;
[`portabilis/pre-matricula-digital`](https://github.com/portabilis/pre-matricula-digital)
— **AGPL-3.0** (35,326 B, titled in Portuguese), the municipal **pre-enrolment
front door** that sits in front of `i-educar`;
[`thalisantunes/eecri-2026-ia-artesanato`](https://github.com/thalisantunes/eecri-2026-ia-artesanato)
— **MIT**, whose reusable part is a **blind prompt-test harness across five
models**.

🔴 **Two of the eight are licence warnings a LATAM engagement should carry:**
[`comercisandro/RecursoEducativoAbierto`](https://github.com/comercisandro/RecursoEducativoAbierto)
is **CC BY-NC-SA 4.0** — the **NonCommercial** term rules it out for client
work, and its licence is served as a Jekyll page rather than a payload — and
[`amandasiqueirasouza1-ux/ia-para-professores`](https://github.com/amandasiqueirasouza1-ux/ia-para-professores)
has **no `LICENSE` at `HEAD`** (404) while its README promises MIT for code
*"that may be included in future"*: a licence that is a **prediction**, not a
grant. 🔵 **Both are the ordinary texture of Portuguese- and Spanish-language
education OSS**, and neither is visible to an English census at all.

🟢 **The pitch this changes.** This file's LATAM sections have argued the
region is strong on demand and thin on reusable supply. The demand evidence
stands (Cetic.br: **53 %** of Brazilian school administrators using AI against
**22 %** with guidelines; UNESCO IESALC: **87 %** / **26 %** across 19
countries). 🔴 **The supply half needs restating: it was thin in this base's
index, not in the region**, and the honest version of the pitch is that the
components exist, are permissively licensed more often than not, and are
small enough that a studio's value is integration and hardening rather than
invention.

**🆕 `p540` — stop selling adoption; sell evidence.** UNESCO IESALC, 200
institutions across 19 countries: **87 %** use AI somewhere, **74 %** in
teaching and learning, **26 %** hold a formal AI strategy, and **9 %** evaluate
what their AI use delivers. 🔵 **Ninety-one percent of institutions that have
already spent the money cannot say whether it worked** — and unlike an adoption
pitch, that buyer has a budget line to defend. The supply is already shelved:
[`EducationalTestingService/skll`](https://github.com/EducationalTestingService/skll)
(BSD-3-Clause, from the organisation behind TOEFL and the GRE) for the
experiment harness, the causal-inference layer `p535` filed for the estimator,
and `xapipe` for the learner-record feed. 🟢 **Counterparts now have names:**
the **Observatory on AI in Education for LAC**, launched **14 April 2026**, is
UNESCO-led with CAF, CENIA, Cetic.br, ECLAC, Fundación Ceibal, Fundación
Santillana, Tecnológico de Monterrey, ProFuturo, Universidad del Desarrollo and
IRCAI. 🟡 Brazil corroborates at school level: 53 % of administrators use AI,
22 % have guidelines. 🔵 **And part of this region's apparent supply blank is
this base's own instrument** — `Gap 540-B` measured that the grant classifier
misgrades Portuguese-language licence files.

🆕 **`p539` — LATAM now has the best-evidenced adoption number in this entire
file, and it is a governance gap rather than an adoption gap.**

🟢 **UNESCO IESALC with UNU-IAS, launched during Digital Learning Week 2026,
surveyed 200 institutions across 19 countries:**

| Measure | Value |
|---|---|
| institutions using AI in at least one area | 🟢 **87 %** |
| institutions with a **formal AI strategy** | 🔴 **26 %** |
| using AI in teaching and learning | **74 %** |

🔵 **That 87-against-26 split is the sharpest engagement opening in this
document** — and it beats the comparable North American figure this base already
holds (~10 % of institutions with formal AI guidelines) on evidence quality,
because it is a primary multi-country survey by a UN body rather than a vendor
poll. 🔴 **And the report's own causal note is the part to sell against:**
adoption is driven by **individual academics, researchers and students**, not by
institution-wide strategy. The deliverable is therefore governance and
consolidation of tools already in use, not a greenfield build.

**Institutional and regulatory holdings read this pass:**

- 🟢 **UNESCO launched the Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and
  the Caribbean on 14 April**, at **ECLAC** headquarters in Santiago during the
  2026 regional sustainable-development forum. A named regional platform for
  governments is a partner to route an engagement through.
- **Uruguay** — first country in LATAM to sign the Council of Europe's
  **Framework Convention on AI** (2025).
- **Colombia** — national AI policy **CONPES 4144**, adopted Feb 2025.
- **Mexico** — federal AI bill in the Senate since 2024; risk-based, proposes an
  oversight authority. 🟡 Status unconfirmed.
- 🔴 **Brazil — PL 2338 was stalled as of the 2024 analysis this base read, and
  its current status could not be confirmed this pass.** Recorded as unknown, not
  as stalled.
- 🔴 **A 2026 Universidad de los Andes study counts 716 AI-related regulatory
  instruments, rules and proposals across 23 countries and territories** — and
  one commentary reads that volume as producing *uncertainty* rather than
  governance. 🔵 Which is consistent with the 26 % strategy figure: the region is
  not short of rules, it is short of institutional capacity to apply them.

**Named actors:** UNESCO, UNESCO IESALC, UNU-IAS, ECLAC, FLACSO Ecuador,
**Tecnológico de Monterrey** (which signed an agreement with UNESCO on AI in
education), and civil-society groups **Derechos Digitales**, **Coding Rights**
and **Fundación Karisma**.

🟢 **Supply, and it keeps the region's one consistent strength:** every LATAM row
this base has filed is **anchored to a national standard** rather than to a
prompt. 🆕 [`GuilhermeScarpini/agente-bncc-professores`](https://github.com/GuilhermeScarpini/agente-bncc-professores)
(MIT, 🔴 0★) generates lesson plans and answer-keyed exams against **official
BNCC competency codes**, sourcing the standard from the **MEC**'s own PDF, with
semantic search over it. That is the same silhouette as `corretor-enem`'s
calibration against 11 147 examiner-marked ENEM essays. 🔴 **At 0★ and 0 forks it
is an architecture to read, not a dependency to take** — and the region still has
no education artefact above three figures of stars.

🔴 **Declared gaps for this region, unchanged or new:** the legs returned
**nothing on K-12 AI policy** and **nothing on current private-sector edtech
players** in LATAM. Both were searched and both came back empty. That is an
informed gap, not coverage.

> 🆕 **`p538` — the region gains a third agent-leg artefact and, more usefully,
> a named incumbent.**
> [`maiatechdev/avit`](https://github.com/maiatechdev/avit) (**MIT**, 0★) is a
> Brazilian classroom-session Socratic tutor: the teacher opens a session by
> code or QR, each student picks a path (*Investigar · Criar · Resolver ·
> Colaborar*), and the tutor gives a **ladder of hints without revealing the
> answer**, explaining the steps only after three unsuccessful attempts. 🟢 **The
> session model is the transferable part** — it assumes a teacher in the room,
> which matches the 74 % classroom-adoption figure above far better than the
> self-serve tutors this base mostly holds. ⚠️ README states no real student data
> until a legal review completes.
>
> 🟢 **And [`shishiv/EDUCA`](https://github.com/shishiv/EDUCA) (MIT, 1★) names
> what this region's districts actually run:** *"A planilha da secretaria de
> educação não aguenta mais"* — the spreadsheet the education secretariat runs
> on cannot cope. 🔵 **That is the most commercially useful sentence this pass
> found for LATAM**, and no market report above contains it. The competitor in a
> *secretaria* engagement is not Moodle or an incumbent vendor; it is a
> spreadsheet, which means the sale is **first-time digitisation**, not
> replacement — different buyer, different objection, different price. ⚠️ EDUCA
> itself is synthetic-data-only and not production-approved.
>
> 🟡 **The region's standing shape is unchanged and should not be overstated:**
> its three agent-leg artefacts total **7 stars and 0 releases**
> (`corretor-enem` 7★, `avit` 0★, plus the pre-release `tutor-ai-agent`), while
> its shipped open-source strength remains school management —
> [`portabilis/i-educar`](https://github.com/portabilis/i-educar). **High
> adoption, thin buildable supply** remains the LATAM thesis.

> [`JustinoLM/STI-matematicas`](https://github.com/JustinoLM/STI-matematicas) —

> 🆕 **`p534` — the governance gap is now corroborated by a second, independent

> is consolidating them — not writing the twentieth SAES scraper. 🟡 And
> [`menthorlabs/menthor`](https://github.com/menthorlabs/menthor) (Apache-2.0,
> **582★**, Brazil) is **archived**: a design reference and a migration source,
> not a platform.

**Regulatory posture — fragmented, and now measured.** 🆕 A 2026 **Universidad de
los Andes** study counts **716 AI-related instruments across 23 countries and
territories, more than 80% of them from 2023 onward** — the scale of the churn,
quantified. No regional AI law exists. Country detail:

- **Brazil** — **PL 2338/2023** at an advanced legislative stage, draft includes
  education provisions on AI literacy and reskilling; the data protection
  authority (**ANPD**) is running an **AI regulatory sandbox pilot through
  December 2026**; Brazil signed an AI and tech regulation agreement with the EU
  in June 2026.
- 🆕 **Uruguay** — **first Latin American country to sign the Council of Europe's
  AI convention** (2025). The region's strongest international-alignment signal.
- 🆕 **Peru** — framework with **regulatory sandboxes and a phased rollout**.
- 🆕 **Colombia** — national AI policy **CONPES 4144**, adopted February 2025.
- 🆕 **Mexico** — federal, risk-based AI bill **in the Senate**; still **no
  comprehensive AI law in force**, and the National AI Strategy dates to 2018.
- **Argentina** — the president submitted a bill that would leave AI
  *unregulated*, the opposite direction from Brazil.

ALSUR characterises the whole picture as *legislative reactivity without a
comprehensive legal framework*; commentators warn the overlapping initiatives are
themselves a source of uncertainty.

**Adoption.** 🆕 The **UNESCO IESALC + UNU-IAS** study of **200 institutions in 19
countries** is the best-evidenced regional figure now available: **87% use AI
somewhere, only 26% have a formal AI strategy**, adoption driven by **individual
faculty and students rather than institutional policy**, with public institutions
more resource-constrained than private. The IDB separately reviewed **193 regional
initiatives**: **59% used generative AI, 27% used language models**, concluding the
region *recombines* AI tools in its own ways rather than importing them wholesale.
Counterweight: **CENIA (Chile) finds 13 of 19 LAC countries studied have not yet
introduced early AI adoption in schools**, and adoption in urban schools is
concentrated in better-resourced and private institutions, with rural and
Indigenous-language contexts underserved. **Google.org has committed $4.6M to AI
literacy across nine countries including Brazil and Mexico, targeting 1.25M
students by 2028.**

**Players.** 🆕 **UNESCO is the most active institutional actor**: it launched the
**Observatory on Artificial Intelligence in Education for Latin America and the
Caribbean in Santiago on 14 April 2026**, runs capacity-building for officials in
Ecuador and Chile, and has an agreement with **Tecnológico de Monterrey** on AI in
education. **ECLAC** coordinates a regional AI working group with **Chile's CENIA**
and **Uruguay's AGESIC**. The World Bank, UNICEF and regional development banks
fund most lower-income pilots; **Derechos Digitales** and **Fundación Karisma**
are the digital-rights counterweight. From the 2026 Holon IQ LATAM EdTech 100:
**Teachy** (Brazil, AI lesson preparation — a regional incumbent that has expanded
into Asia), **Ginia** (Mexico, employability), **Vetto** (Brazil), **Mattilda**
(Mexico, billing for private schools). The list shows AI positioning shifting
**toward teachers**, with upskilling losing ground as a category.

**Open-source anchors, placed:** [i-educar](https://github.com/portabilis/i-educar)
(GPL-2.0, 722★) — Brazil's largest free school-management system, born in Itajaí
(SC) municipal government, on the federal Public Software Portal since 2008 — plus
[Chamilo](https://github.com/chamilo/chamilo-lms) and
[RosarioSIS](https://github.com/francoisjacquet/rosariosis) for ES/PT
localisation. 🆕 **And one asset that is LATAM *data* rather than LATAM code:**
[pedagogy-benchmark](https://github.com/AI-for-Education/pedagogy-benchmark)
(MIT) is built on exams from **Chile's Agencia de la Calidad de la Educación and
CPEIP**, credited on the repository. The region's teaching standards are already
inside an open benchmark — that is leverage nobody is using.

**Opportunities.**
1. **Teacher-facing first, mirroring where the market already moved.** Teachy's
   position and the EdTech 100 both say the buyer is the teacher, not the learner.
   Lesson preparation and feedback in Portuguese and Spanish, built on
   [Educhain](https://github.com/satvik314/educhain) (MIT) or 🆕
   [lineage-skill](https://github.com/JuneYaooo/lineage-skill) (Apache-2.0,
   source-backed) emitting
   [H5P](https://github.com/h5p/h5p-php-library) for portability.
2. **The i-educar modernisation engagement.** Hundreds of Brazilian municipalities
   run GPL-2.0 school management from 2008. The work is an integration layer —
   enrolment, attendance and performance signals into an advising agent — not a
   replacement, and GPL-2.0 means it must stay across a service boundary.
3. **Use the ANPD sandbox as the de-risking vehicle** — and now Peru's too, which
   also has sandboxes. A pilot inside Brazil's regulatory sandbox before December
   2026 produces a compliance story that survives PL 2338 becoming law. That is a
   reason to start now rather than wait.
4. 🆕 **Sell the strategy, not the tool — the 87/26 split is the pitch.** UNESCO
   IESALC says 87% of LATAM higher-ed institutions already use AI and only 26%
   have a strategy, with adoption driven bottom-up by individual faculty. The
   institutions do not need to be sold AI; they need **the governance layer over
   the AI already in their buildings**: inventory, policy, human-in-the-loop
   enforcement, xAPI audit trail via [Ralph](https://github.com/openfun/ralph)
   (MIT). This is a consulting-led engagement with a software tail, and it is the
   highest-probability LATAM opening on this file.
5. 🆕 **Build the Chilean benchmark into a Spanish-language evaluation offer.**
   CDPK/SEND over Chile's own teacher-certification exams, in Spanish, is a
   credible answer to "does this model know how to teach *here*" — and it is MIT.
   Pair it with CENIA, which already co-chairs the ECLAC working group.
6. **Thirteen of nineteen countries have not started.** CENIA's number is the
   greenfield read: national AI-literacy programmes with no incumbent to displace.
   Kolibri (MIT, offline-first) is the right base where connectivity binds.

#### 🆕 Two additions from `p528`'s sweep

🟢 **Teacher-formation is organising regionally, and it is the entry point.** In
**October 2026** UNESCO convened **faculties of education from across Latin
America in Santiago** to work on how to prepare future teachers for AI, with
experiences presented from **Mexico, Colombia and Chile**. 🔵 **Why this is
commercially relevant and not just news:** it is the same institutional circle
that runs the **LAC Observatory on AI in Education** (launched 14 Apr 2026,
Santiago — CAF, CENIA, Cetic.br, CEPAL, Fundación Ceibal, Tecnológico de
Monterrey), and Trend 10 on [`trends.md`](trends.md) says the buyer is moving
from the learner to the teacher. **Teacher formation is where regional
procurement is being designed, in public, right now.**

🟡 **One deployment figure, date-flagged.** A regional analysis reports **Letrus
used in 400 Brazilian schools**, and Chile's **Elige Educar** applying AI to
teacher retention and recruitment. ⚠️ **The source is dated 2025**, so treat both
as the most recent *published* figures rather than current ones; neither was
confirmed against the vendors this pass.

🔴 **And the honest counterweight, unchanged:** the same analysis warns that LATAM
education systems are absorbing AI tools **often without oversight or
guidelines** — which is the demand side of pattern **P554**, not a reason to
discount it.

#### 🆕 `p529` — LATAM: the best-sourced regional dataset this base holds

🟢 **UNESCO IESALC — 200 institutions across 19 countries.** The first LATAM
figure on this file with a stated sample frame rather than a vendor estimate:

| Measure | Value |
|---|---|
| Institutions using AI in **at least one** area | 🟢 **87%** |
| …with a **formal AI strategy** | 🔴 **26.0%** |
| …with **institution-wide policies** | 🔴 **18.5%** |
| …with a **dedicated AI budget** | 🔴 **8.0%** |
| …with **formal evaluation mechanisms** | 🔴 **9.0%** |
| Use in **teaching and learning** | 73.5% |
| Use in **research** | 57.0% |

⚠️ **Second source, not comparable:** the Digital Education Council's *AI in
Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026* reports **92% of students** and **79% of
faculty** actively engaging with AI — but across **29 institutions**. Different
frame, different unit. Shown beside the UNESCO figures, **not averaged with them**.

- **Regulation:** no unified regional framework. **Chile** leads — national AI
  policy since **2021**, with a law under discussion; **Brazil** and **Colombia**
  have advanced national strategies **without education-specific rules**;
  **Mexico**'s SEP, ANUIES and the Observatorio IA have issued recommendations
  that are **non-binding**. One regional study concludes development is
  *heterogeneous*. ⚠️ Chile's bill status in 2026 was not confirmed this pass.
- **Institutional actors to name in a proposal:** **UNESCO IESALC** (Caracas),
  the **Digital Education Council**, **Tecnológico de Monterrey** with its
  Institute for the Future of Education, **UNAM**, **UPC Peru**, **PUC Chile**.
  Private non-profit universities lead adoption.
- 🔵 **The gap between 87% and 8% is the entire LATAM opportunity, and it is not
  a tooling gap.** Institutions have already adopted; **92% of them have no
  dedicated budget and 91% have no way to evaluate what they adopted.** Selling
  another tutor into that is selling into the 87%. **Selling evaluation,
  policy scaffolding and procurement-grade evidence is selling into the 74-92%
  who cannot yet govern what they run** — and it is exactly what
  [`AI-for-Education/pedagogy-benchmark`](https://github.com/AI-for-Education/pedagogy-benchmark)
  (MIT) and `k12-teacher-skills`' rubrics are for.
- 🟢 **And LATAM ships code, which this base denied for seven passes.** Beyond
  `corretor-enem` (Brazil), the census head holds **five** LATAM-origin Moodle
  bridges — **USP** (Brazil) and **FICH-UNL, UTN, UTN FRLP, Universidad de San
  Andrés** (Argentina). 🔴 **More than any other region, including North America
  at zero.** The old note — *"the LATAM contribution is data, not code"* — is now
  falsified twice over and should not reappear.


#### 🆕 `p530` — `Gap 529-D` CONFIRMED OPEN, and the regional instruments now have numbers

🔴 **`Gap 529-D` stands exactly as `p529` worded it.** A fourth targeted sweep
found **no AI-in-education market sizing for LATAM on a methodology comparable to
the per-geography reports published for North America, Europe and APAC.** 🔵
Every cross-regional comparison on this file remains **not like-for-like for
LATAM** — the region this base is asked about most. Closing it needs a paid
report or ministry statistics, **not another search**; four passes have now
confirmed that.

🟢 **What the sweep did add — the policy-to-budget gap, quantified.**

| Finding | Source | Figure |
|---|---|---|
| National AI instruments that **explicitly allocate budget** | 2026 regional AI index | 🔴 **only Brazil and Colombia** |
| Large enterprises with ≥ 1 AI project in production | IDB / McKinsey, via regional reporting | 🟢 **67%** |
| …reporting **measurable** impact on business metrics | same | 🔴 **23%** |
| Higher-ed institutions using AI | UNESCO IESALC — **200 institutions, 19 countries** | 🟢 87% |
| …with a strategy / a budget / an evaluation framework | same | 🔴 **26% / 8% / 9%** |

🔵 **Two independent instruments, one shape.** Enterprise: 67% building, 23%
measuring. Higher education: 87% using, **8% funding**, 9% evaluating. 🟢 **The
regional bottleneck is not adoption and not regulation — it is the step from
pilot to funded, measured programme.** That is a consulting-shaped gap, and it is
the same conclusion from two unrelated datasets, which is why it is worth
printing.

🟡 **Regulatory state, unchanged from what this base holds.** Brazil PL
2.338/2023 (risk-based, in the Chamber of Deputies, text can still change);
Colombia CONPES **4144** (Feb 2025, policy-first); Mexico still has **no
AI-specific statute**; Colombia, Peru and Paraguay have proposals naming
education explicitly. 🔴 **Chile's 2026 bill status remains unconfirmed, and
Canada and Mexico education policy returned nothing for the sixth consecutive
pass.**

🟢 **LATAM supply verified this pass** — and it is no longer only data:
[`LabSirius/TutorIA`](https://github.com/LabSirius/TutorIA) (MIT, **Universidad
Tecnológica de Pereira**, rural higher education in Risaralda, Colombia — a
stated-origin university agent, 0★ so a reference not a component),
[`Mcp-Brasil/mcp-brasil`](https://github.com/Mcp-Brasil/mcp-brasil) (MIT,
**1 840★**, MCP server over 70 Brazilian public APIs — the highest-starred
LATAM-origin address this base has recorded),
[`cortega26/chile-hub`](https://github.com/cortega26/chile-hub) (MIT, Chilean
public data including education, with a per-dataset redistribution audit),
[`dsvi-b/corretor-enem`](https://github.com/dsvi-b/corretor-enem) (MIT, ENEM
essay marking calibrated on 11 147 real essays).


#### 🆕 `p542` — one absent instrument found, and a supply blank reported as a blank

- 🔴 **IDB *fAIr Tech Radar* (November 2025) — absent from this base entirely.**
  *Exploring the Adoption of Artificial Intelligence in Latin America and the
  Caribbean*, from the Inter-American Development Bank, characterises adoption
  as advancing in a context marked by **structural gaps, developing regulatory
  frameworks and heterogeneous digital capabilities**. **`unverified-at-primary`.**
  🔵 **Why it belongs on this page:** this base's LATAM case has rested on
  market-size forecasts and the ECLAC leader ranking. An **IDB** instrument is
  a *development-bank* reading, which is the frame regional ministries and
  multilateral buyers actually fund against — a different and more bankable
  authority than a vendor forecast.
- 🟡 **UNU + UNESCO IESALC working paper (September 2026)** maps AI adoption
  across LAC universities along **teaching, research, community engagement,
  administration and governance**. **`unverified-at-primary`**; IESALC itself is
  already held here. 🟢 **The five-axis split is the useful part** — it gives an
  engagement a diagnostic shape, and *administration* and *governance* are the
  two axes this base's LATAM patterns have never addressed.
- 🟡 **ECLAC (October 2025): Chile, Brazil and Mexico lead**, with gaps in
  data, training and regulation; Baker McKenzie notes member states are at
  **different stages** of AI regulatory development. Consistent with what this
  base holds.
- 🔴 **Supply: blank, and this is the result worth recording.** **No
  LATAM-origin artefact surfaced on any leg this pass** — including the Spanish
  and Portuguese re-runs, which produced **eight new artefacts for `p541` one
  pass earlier.** 🔵 **Two readings, and this pass cannot separate them:**
  either `p541` exhausted the readily-reachable Iberian-language supply, or
  the single-pass sample is simply too small to be stable. 🟢 **Either way the
  honest statement is that LATAM's agent-leg supply on this base still rests on
  `p541`'s three artefacts plus `avit`, and nothing was added.** **That is an
  informed blank, not coverage** — and it is the opposite of the region's
  *demand* picture, which remains the best-evidenced of the four.

## Declared gaps this pass

Written down because an unstated gap is indistinguishable from coverage.

### 🆕 `p534` additions

- 🔴 **EMEA classroom-adoption demand is a measured blank for the fourth
  consecutive pass, and the diagnosis has moved from the window to the query.**
  `AI education EMEA {year} adoption regulation players` returns enterprise-AI
  material because **"EMEA" is a vendor's sales territory, not a term education
  ministries or researchers use about themselves.** 🟢 **The remedy is to stop
  running it as one leg:** query the named instruments and the named
  jurisdictions — **OECD Digital Education Outlook**, **Eurydice**/Eurostat
  digital-education indicators, and country terms (*Bildung*, *éducation*,
  *onderwijs*) — rather than a regional acronym. 🔵 **Cost:** one pass. 🔴 **And
  the mandated leg must still be run and reported**, because its emptiness is
  itself the evidence for this gap.
- 🔴 **North America's regional sizing and the global band are irreconcilable
  and both are carried.** $951M (2024) → $2 303.2M (2029) at **15.9 %** for
  North America sits under a global 2026 band of **$3.7B–$10.6B** at
  **21–41 %** — i.e. the region said to hold **36 % of the market** is sized at
  roughly a tenth of it. 🟢 One of the two is measuring a narrower segment and
  neither says which. **Until that is resolved, no slide should put a regional
  and a global figure side by side.**
- 🟡 **The Canadian position is unresolved.** **AIDA** (the Artificial
  Intelligence and Data Act) is described as *proposing* a foundation for
  responsible AI, and 🔴 **nothing this leg returned confirms it is law.** It is
  carried as a bill, not a statute, and a Canadian engagement needs the current
  status read first-hand.
- 🔴 **Three of this pass's four North American supply finds have 0 stars**, so
  no popularity-ranked instrument will ever surface them. The regional supply
  legs have been looking for *trending* repositories in a sector whose
  institutional supply does not trend. → see `Gap 534-C` on
  [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md).

### 🆕 `p533` additions

- 🔴 **EMEA classroom-adoption demand is a measured blank for the third
  consecutive pass**, and **APAC's for the second**. Both legs return
  regulation in depth and adoption not at all. 🔵 Closing either needs a named
  instrument rather than another query: for EMEA the **OECD Digital Education
  Outlook** and Eurostat/Eurydice digital-education indicators; for APAC a
  national ministry series. 🔴 Until then, every cross-regional *adoption*
  sentence in this file is grounded for **North America and LATAM only**, and
  the other two legs are inference.
- 🟡 **The North American bill count is still unresolved and now has a third
  value.** `p532` held *28+DC vs 34+DC+PR*; this pass's leg returned
  *134 bills across 31 states in 2026*. Three mutually inconsistent framings
  (enacted vs introduced vs bill-count) from secondary trackers. **No figure
  here is quotable without a primary legislature source**, and that is the
  honest state rather than a number to pick.
- 🔴 **The split-grant problem has no instrument.** `bncc-benchmark` proves a
  single repository can carry two grants scoped by path, and this base grades
  one grant per address. Until `Gap 533-B` closes, **any dataset-bearing
  repository on these pages may be mis-graded** — and dataset repositories are
  disproportionately where LATAM's contribution sits.

### 🆕 `p529` additions

- 🔴 **No LATAM market sizing on the same methodology as the other three
  regions.** MarketsandMarkets publishes per-geography AI-in-education reports
  for **North America, Europe and APAC** and **none for LATAM**. Every
  cross-regional comparison in this file is therefore **not like-for-like for
  LATAM**, and no amount of care with the other three fixes it. 🔵 Closing it
  needs a paid report or a national-ministry source, not another search.
- 🔴 **Chile's AI bill status in 2026 unconfirmed.** The national policy dates to
  2021 and a law is *"under discussion"*; this pass could not establish whether
  it advanced. It is the region's most likely first binding instrument, so the
  gap matters more than its size suggests.
- 🟡 **The UK £200m+ and EU €25.4m figures rest on one sector newsletter.**
  Primary sources (HM Treasury, the Commission's grant register) were not
  checked. Recorded with the channel named.
- 🟡 **NA's "10% of institutions have formal AI guidelines" and "71% of teachers
  untrained" come from a trade roundup with no published methodology.** They
  point the same way as Gallup's 60%, already on this file from a stronger
  source, which is why they are recorded at all.
- 🔴 **Canada and Mexico education-specific AI policy returned nothing again** —
  **fifth** consecutive pass. The NA leg remains, in substance, a US leg. 🔵
  Saturated at this search depth; moving it needs ministry sources in French and
  Spanish, which this base has never attempted.
- 🟢 **`p528`'s "regional legs are saturated" is withdrawn as stated.** They were
  saturated on *statutes*. Re-querying the same four legs for **surveys and
  budget commitments** returned new attributable material in **all four**. The
  lesson is about the query, not the regions.

- **No authoritative ranking of EMEA AI-education vendors.** The funding figures
  above come from trade aggregators that disagree with each other on round sizes
  and company locations. No primary announcements were checked.
- **North America: Canada and Mexico education-specific AI policy returned almost
  nothing again.** Searched this pass; the regional overviews cite Canada's
  *workplace* AI rules, not education policy. **The NA leg is effectively a US
  leg**, for the second consecutive pass.
- 🆕 **EMEA: no national-level detail for the UK, Germany, France, Saudi Arabia
  or the UAE**, and **no adoption data for Africa at all** — not thin data,
  *absent* data. The EMEA leg is an EU-regulation leg plus a funding list. A pass
  targeting the UK DfE, UAE KHDA and specific African education ministries would
  fix it; this pass did not.
- 🟢 🆕 **RETIRED: "no LATAM-origin open-source education agent."** This gap was
  false when it was written. [`dsvi-b/corretor-enem`](https://github.com/dsvi-b/corretor-enem)
  (**MIT**, Brazil — ENEM's five competencies over 11,147 examiner-marked essays,
  UFPE methodology) was verified by this base at **`p420` on 24 Oct** and never
  shelved; a second MIT Brazilian artefact,
  [`ThallesYVasconcelos/Agente-Educacional`](https://github.com/ThallesYVasconcelos/Agente-Educacional)
  (BNCC/PCN-grounded agentic RAG, 0★), was verified this pass. 🔴 **The gap was in
  this base's promotion path, not in the region's supply** — see
  [`../agents/top.md` § Promotion debt](../agents/top.md). 🟡 **The real LATAM gap,
  restated:** the region's two verified agent-leg artefacts total **7 stars and
  zero releases**. The shortage is *maturity and adoption*, not origin. What LATAM
  also contributes is **data** (Chile → CDPK/SEND).
- 🆕 **The regional policy legs are saturated at this search depth.** `p528` ran
  all four regional queries and every NA/EMEA/APAC item returned was already on
  this base. **Stated so the next pass does not spend its budget here:** moving
  these legs now needs *primary* sources — the UK DfE, UAE KHDA, named African
  education ministries, Canada's and Mexico's education departments, and
  Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 read from EUR-Lex — not another round of regional
  search.
- **The "under 10% of schools have AI rules" figure has no primary citation.**
- **APAC curriculum status is internally contradictory across sources** (see the
  warning above) and needs per-country verification before client use.
- **Market-size figures are vendor/blog-sourced**, not read from the research
  firms' own reports. The 30-35% CAGR consensus is the quotable part; the point
  estimates are not — and the band widened this pass rather than narrowing.
- 🆕 **The EU public-authority 2 Aug 2030 date comes from one commentary source**
  among the several that agree on 2 Dec 2027. It is the most commercially
  consequential line in the EMEA section and the least corroborated — **read
  Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 itself before putting that date in front of a public
  client.**
